UNVERIFIED: A restore that runs past claimOrphanTimeout (5 min) can have its claim drained by the WPC GC mid-restore. This predates 0092 and is bounded by the `resuming` watchdog (300s, with the OD-1 anchor drift). The §7.3 restore area owns it, not this proposal.

### [spec.1.review-mechanism.1]

FACT: §10.1.2 step 0 tells the acquiring replica to read the row for `$expected_generation` immediately before the CAS; nothing in spec ties that read to the read that found an adoptable state. Decision 17 (CAS on the listed generation) exists only in CODE-5 item 2b. EVIDENCE: spec/10_gateway-internals.md:36-37; non-spec-changes.md:112
WATCHOUT: The staged §10.1.5 **Session with no bound pod.** paragraph says "A compare-and-swap that lands after the attempt's write ends that incarnation". Under SPEC-2a that CAS lands only on a `resuming` row, which SPEC-2a says is never adopted. Fix it by naming the step-0 read as the adoption read. Do not add a handoff CAS to the **Exits from `resuming`.** list. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:132, :146
FACT: Only the stream-holding (coordinating) replica can evaluate "no turn in flight", and the `last_agent_activity_at` guard cannot see a silent turn whose admission write is older than the idle window. SPEC-5f says only "the gateway". EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:434; summary.md:40
WATCHOUT: SPEC-5i states two opposite outcomes for a delivery that fails after a commit to `running`. Synchronous delivery re-reads the session and routes the message again. The drain drops the message unless the failure was a refusal. Bring both to one rule, stated in **Delivery after a commit to `running`.** EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:475, :479
WATCHOUT: Under `durableInbox: true` the drain's "at most once" and "returns to the DLQ" rules conflict with the durable-inbox Explicit ACK row (remove only after delivery, at-least-once). EVIDENCE: spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:305 (Explicit ACK row), :312 (guarantee)
FACT: The §4.2 session-records bullet and the §10.1.1 **Generation counters:** bullet both carry the full `coordination_generation` progression (baseline 1 plus both incrementers) after SPEC-1f items 1 and 2. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:167; spec/10_gateway-internals.md:30
FACT: Message custody for the held-pod/off-holder, podless, resume-commit, and idle-refusal edges traced clean: each message is delivered once or expires through the DLQ TTL or terminal drain. The two exceptions are the failed-delivery divergence above and the RECORDS-1 7(f) and 7(g) residues the summary already files.

### [spec.1.review-performance.1]
FACT: Tier 3 budgets for this lens: Redis coordination concern ~10K ops/s single-tenant, Postgres sustained write ~1,300/s and burst ~3,900/s, REG-COORDLEASE TTL 60 s, Sweeper interval 15 s, watchdog 5 s. EVIDENCE: spec/17_deployment-topology.md:1176-1177, :1201; spec/28_communication-channels.md:137; pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:197
WATCHOUT: Do not re-file lease or backlog-probe scaling with the 7-day suspended population. The material skeptic refuted it in r2-r5 of the earlier run ("capacity concern about a deliberate design choice", no spec-stated population bound). A per-suspended-session DLQ probe on each 5 s watchdog tick (SPEC-5i **Backlog resume.**) belongs to the same family. EVIDENCE: scratchpad/cp-log/0092_new_.../history/spec.r5.history.md:52-60
DECISION: Filed two failure-mode findings. (1) The SPEC-5f admission write makes Postgres a synchronous dependency of every message delivery, and the staged text states no outcome when that write fails. §12.3 says existing sessions continue through a failover of up to 30 s, and CODE-6 item 5.2 fails `Send` on any admitter error. (2) SPEC-5i makes the Redis DLQ the only custody for undelivered `suspended`-target messages and puts a DLQ drain in front of synchronous held-pod delivery, but states no outcome for a failed enqueue or drain read. The receipt enum's `inbox_unavailable` is scoped to `durableInbox: true`. — BECAUSE each one is a store outage that degrades below the shipped behavior, with no outcome stated in the staged spec — ALTERNATIVES: suspended-population scaling (refuted earlier); the cost of the admission write (OD-A is open).
FACT: CODE-6 item 5.2 has `Send` return the admitter's error and write no envelope. A Postgres failover therefore fails every message delivery to every running session, on every replica. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:134; spec/12_storage-architecture.md:153

### [spec.1.review-plan-conformance.1]
FACT: The plan entry this proposal claims is the unticked "Session suspend-and-resume proposal" item in gateway-runtime-comms-remediation.md §10.2 Proposal track (not an R-numbered step). It lists: resume driver, owner idle definition, idle→suspension with immediate checkpoint and immediate release (15 min default), any message resumes, 7-day bounded suspension, maxSessionAgeSeconds active time, REG-COORDLEASE lifecycle in resume_pending/awaiting_client_action plus the §29.3 /resume forward, retry-budget reconciliation, five spec gaps, and F-11.3.35/F-11.3.36. EVIDENCE: gateway-runtime-comms-remediation.md:2474-2522
FACT: Every plan deliverable is staged in spec-changes.md or consciously declined: the §29.3 /resume forward is replaced by the store-only route (summary Non-goals), and the expired-pod recycle gap is handed to the recycling follow-up (summary Defects; RECORDS-1 item 9). Spec-loop plan-conformance returned no findings in round 1.
WATCHOUT: §29.2 step 11 (spec/29_communication-scenarios.md:208-214), which the plan cites for "no acquisition point", concerns lease acquisition at session creation, not the recovering states; SPEC-2a does not falsify it, so it is not a missed edit site.

### [spec.1.review-reliability.1]
WATCHOUT: SPEC-5i states two different rules for a delivery that fails ambiguously. **Synchronous delivery.** re-routes it by the current state, which can deliver it twice. **Delivery after a commit to `running`.** keeps it out, because the runtime may already have it. One rule should be the home and the other should cite it. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:475, :479
WATCHOUT: **Enqueue after a drain.** covers only a re-read that finds `running` or `input_required`. An enqueue that lands after a terminal transition's DLQ drain (§7.3 "DLQ drain on terminal transition", spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:444) is neither delivered nor expired, because the DLQ expiry sweep lists only non-terminal rows. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:478
FACT: §6.2 defines idle time as the time elapsed since `last_agent_activity_at` (spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:265, :277), so a "Paused" row cannot keep its accumulated idle time. CODE-6 item 8 stamps on entry to `running` (non-spec-changes.md:144), and no staged spec text states that stamp.
FACT: The orphan-claim GC keys on `pod_assignment` (pkg/controller/warmpool/gc.go:194-240). CODE-4 defers `PodAssignment` until the commit, so a driver's claimed pod becomes GC-eligible after `claimOrphanTimeout` (default 5 min). The `resuming` timeout default is also 300 s. Not filed: the exposure window is narrow, and the GC also acts as the reclaimer for a stalled attempt.
UNVERIFIED: On a concurrent pod (`maxConcurrentSessions > 1`), a crash between the SPEC-5b binding clear and the slot release leaves the Redis slot counter incremented. Neither the orphan GC (keyed per pod, and other sessions still reference the pod) nor counter rehydration (which runs only after a Redis restart) reclaims it. The adapter's coordinator-hold timeout may reclaim the slot on the adapter side, and the gateway counter remains unchecked. A Kubernetes or performance lens should check this.

### [spec.1.review-security.1]
FACT: The zero-hold idle release (SPEC-5b step 2) reuses the existing session-end disposition, so tenant pinning, the acknowledgeProcessLevelIsolation admission rule, and maxSessionsPerPod counting still govern the released pod; no security control is bypassed by idle suspension. EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:448, :525, :560
FACT: The awaiting_client_action idle-clock change in SPEC-5e ("Paused") is safe because `runtime.maxAwaitingClientActionSeconds` (900s) already bounds that state. EVIDENCE: spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:443; spec/11_policy-and-controls.md:208
FACT: Budget-key expiry still fails closed (`BUDGET_KEYS_EXPIRED` sentinel check), so raising the TTL default to 8 days and the SPEC-5o re-arm do not let a budget ceiling reset silently. EVIDENCE: spec/08_recursive-delegation.md:406-408
DECISION: The SPEC-2b fence skip for a podless adoptee is not a fence bypass — BECAUSE a binding appears only on a resuming→running commit conditioned on the incarnation generation, and CODE-5 item 2b makes RecordHandoff a CAS on the generation of the listed row, so the "no bound pod" read and the CAS expectation come from one row version — ALTERNATIVES: filing a requirement that the no-bound-pod check use the step-0 read (rejected: the race needs the binding to be read separately from the CAS expectation, and the staged code does not do that).
WATCHOUT: Every message to a `suspended` session resuming it (SPEC-5i) is not a privilege escalation: before this change any sender could already resume one with `delivery: immediate`. EVIDENCE: spec/07_session-lifecycle.md path 6 ("This applies uniformly to all message sources")

### [spec.1.review-single-source.1]
FACT: §4.2's session-records bullet and §10.1.1 **Generation counters:** both already stated the coordination_generation baseline and first-handoff value before this proposal; SPEC-1f items 1 and 2 rewrite both in full, so the progression has two stating sites — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:167; spec/10_gateway-internals.md:30
WATCHOUT: Custody of a delivery refused by the idle-suspension admission is stated twice in the staging: SPEC-5f ("routes it as a message to a `suspended` session") and SPEC-5i **Delivery after a commit to `running`.** ("returns ... with their original scores"). A client interrupt mid-drain makes the drain case reachable. Reduce SPEC-5f to a pointer at path 6 rather than adding a third ordering.
FACT: Remaining fence-precondition sites outside SPEC-2c (CH-CHECKPOINT and CH-BARRIER Preconditions bullets, §28.6 "the fence acknowledgement closes the window") are all pod-bound contexts and do not contradict SPEC-2b's no-pod skip — EVIDENCE: spec/28_communication-channels.md:295, :378, :1820
USEFUL [Standing context, SPEC-5i custody DECISION]: confirmed path 6 is the only full statement of enqueue-before-state-write and the drain order; SPEC-5i2 reduces the SPEC-1b item 4 copy to a pointer.

### [spec.2.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: The SPEC-5i path 6 drain restores the failed DLQ entry with the unattempted ones at their original scores, so the DLQ half is at-least-once like the inbox half — BECAUSE a dropped entry after an ambiguous pod or stream failure is lost with no `message_expired`, breaking the §7.2 promise to senders holding `queued` (spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:350, :352) — ALTERNATIVES: at-most-once plus `message_expired` (no legal reason value; notifies a loss the restore avoids), per-error-kind exceptions (the removed `ErrSessionSuspended` branch), a `delivered_message_ids` clause in the staged text (defends rather than instructs; no adapter code implements the set).
WATCHOUT: Replaced: "A drained DLQ entry is delivered at most once" with the idle-refusal-only restore exception (`TestDeliverBufferedRestoresRefusedEntry`, CODE-6 item 5.4's `deliverBuffered` sentence). It lost because the common post-resume failure (pod failure or stream break) dropped the entry with no sender notification. Do not reintroduce an error-kind branch in `deliverBuffered`; redelivery duplicates are the adapter's `delivered_message_ids` concern (spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:305).
DECISION: `dlq_ttl_expired` carries no state predicate ("pre-terminal DLQ TTL elapsed before the message was delivered") in §7.2 and §15.4 — BECAUSE a restored entry can expire in a `running` session's DLQ, and the pre-running DLQ row (spec/07:348) also expires entries; a state list drifts each time custody gains a state and the enum is closed (spec/15_external-api-surface.md:1641) — ALTERNATIVES: adding `running` to the list, sweeping `running` rows in `sweepDLQExpiry` (a Redis call per running row per tick).
DECISION: The dead-letter recovering-state row's "delivered in FIFO order" sentence is replaced by a pointer at path 6 **Delivery after a commit to `running`.** (SPEC-5z item 4) — BECAUSE the DLQ is scored by expiry, so FIFO and score order disagree when TTLs differ; path 6 is the one home of drain order.
CORRECTS [DECISION SPEC-5z widens ... to a `suspended` target]: SPEC-5z no longer widens `dlq_ttl_expired` to `suspended`; it removes the state predicate. The §7.3 terminal DLQ drain bullet (item 2) still names `suspended`.

### [spec.2.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: SPEC-5d2 deletes the timer-cause glosses at §6.2 **Pod failure while `suspended` (pod still held):** and §10.1 **Orphan session reconciliation:** — BECAUSE both sentences already define their set by pod binding, and after SPEC-5f an idle-suspended session is podless without `maxSuspendedPodHoldSeconds` — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's "before the graceful pod release" (false for an idle session whose release checkpoint keeps failing, §6.2 release step 3); adding the strings to the SPEC-5u sweep (two known sites, explicit edits are exact). EVIDENCE: spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:236; spec/10_gateway-internals.md:59.
OPEN: §6.2 **Pod failure while `suspended` (pod still held):** says recovery returns to `running` "because the interrupt context that caused the original suspension cannot be recovered"; after SPEC-5f an idle-suspended session whose release checkpoint failed also lands here with no interrupt context. Not edited (separate finding). EVIDENCE: spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:236.
DECISION: The pod-named condition on `suspended → running` is stated once, in the SPEC-5i path 6 lead, covering message-triggered and resume-call writes; the copy in **Synchronous delivery.** is deleted — BECAUSE three writers (synchronous delivery, held messages, resume_session/POST resume) need one rule, and the SPEC-5b step 2 release guard plus this condition orders the zero-hold release against all of them — ALTERNATIVES: per-bullet copies (drift); stating the resume-call miss outcome in the lead (pre-empts the open errResumeHeldPodReleased branch decision); release taking the delivery lock (per replica). Code already implements it: non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 item 6 `resumeHeldPod` guard `PodAssignment != ""`.
WATCHOUT: Do not re-add the pod-named condition to **Synchronous delivery.**, **Held messages.**, or the resume paragraph; the path 6 lead governs every bullet.

### [spec.2.fix-G3.1]
DECISION: The SPEC-1f item 1 §4.2 pointer to §10.1.1 **Generation counters:** is now a bare "See [§10.1.1] **Generation counters:**." — BECAUSE the earlier "Its baseline and the writes that advance it are stated in" described the target's contents and was false (the mid-resume terminal bump lives in §7.2 snapshot-close step 4(a), not §10.1.1) — ALTERNATIVES: listing the takeover and resume increments in the pointer (describes the target, goes stale again); adding the terminal bump to staged §10.1.1 (second home for a §7.2 rule).
WATCHOUT: coordination_generation has three advancing writes: takeover CAS and resume_pending → resuming (§10.1.1 as staged) and the mid-resume terminal collapse (§7.2 step 4(a), summarized in the kept remainder of the §4.2 bullet). Do not write a pointer claiming any one site holds all of them. — EVIDENCE: spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:221; spec/04_system-components.md:167

### [spec.2.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: A drained DLQ entry whose delivery fails is returned to the DLQ at its score whatever the failure, so the DLQ drain is at-least-once like the inbox half of the same drain (durable-inbox Explicit ACK row) — BECAUSE dropping it loses a `queued` message with no `message_expired` under the pod or stream failure the drain most often meets, and the refusal exception becomes unnecessary — ALTERNATIVES: keep at-most-once and emit `message_expired` for the dropped entry (needs a fourth enum value; the enum is closed); keep the idle-refusal exception (hair: an error-kind branch the uniform rule makes redundant).
DECISION: `dlq_ttl_expired` is redefined state-independently ("pre-terminal DLQ TTL elapsed before the message was delivered") — BECAUSE a returned entry can expire in a `running` session's DLQ and the drain expires it; listing states again would drift from path 6. This also gives the pre-running DLQ row's expiry a legal reason.
DECISION: The dead-letter recovering-state row's "delivered in FIFO order" sentence is replaced by a pointer to path 6 **Delivery after a commit to `running`.**, the one home of drain order (score order).
CORRECTS [Standing context DECISION "SPEC-5z widens the §7.2 and §15.4 `dlq_ttl_expired` definitions ... to a `suspended` target"]: after this round the two definitions name no state; only the §7.3 terminal-drain bullet (SPEC-5z item 2) and the Target-state cells widen.
WATCHOUT: Do not justify the restore in staged text with the adapter's `delivered_message_ids`; no adapter code implements it (grep of pkg/ cmd/ sdks/ for delivered_message_ids finds nothing) and BUILD-GAPS.md files no finding for it. The inbox path already relies on the same spec MUST (spec/07_session-lifecycle.md Explicit ACK row). EVIDENCE: spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:305
OPEN: The adapter `delivered_message_ids` duplicate suppression mandated by the §7.2 durable-inbox Explicit ACK row is absent from the tree and unfiled in BUILD-GAPS.md; a later round or the RECORDS-1 owner should decide whether 0092 files it.

### [spec.2.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: The two unstaged podless-suspension glosses (§10.1 **Orphan session reconciliation:** "(podless suspension after `maxSuspendedPodHoldSeconds`; see [§6.2]...)" and §6.2 **Pod failure while `suspended` (pod still held):** "(i.e., before `maxSuspendedPodHoldSeconds` fires)") are DELETED as causal glosses, in one new item SPEC-5d2, rather than reworded — BECAUSE "no pod binding" and "the pod is still held" already define the sets by the binding, which is what the §10.1 reconciler and §6.2 release step 3 key on — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's "(the pod was released; ...)" and "(before the graceful pod release)" (rejected: a replacement where a deletion closes it; "before the release" is also false for an idle session whose release checkpoint keeps failing, which holds its pod indefinitely by step 3).
DECISION: The pod-named condition on `suspended → running` is stated once, in the SPEC-5i path 6 lead, covering message- and resume-call-triggered writes; **Synchronous delivery.** drops its local copy — BECAUSE the zero-hold release (SPEC-5b step 2) guards only on `suspended` + bound pod, so an unconditioned running write from **Held messages.** or a resume call can land after it. CODE-6 item 6 (`resumeHeldPod` guard `PodAssignment != ""`, `errResumeHeldPodReleased`) already implements it at every site; no code change.
WATCHOUT: The lead sentence deliberately does NOT state what a resume call does when its running write misses. That outcome is OD-B (`errResumeHeldPodReleased → enterResumePending`, CODE-6 item 6); staging it in spec would pre-empt the open decision. A held message whose write misses is covered by **Backlog resume.**; a synchronous one by **Synchronous delivery.**'s own miss sentence.
OPEN: separate finding for a later round — §6.2 **Pod failure while `suspended` (pod still held):** says recovery lands in `running` "because the interrupt context that caused the original suspension cannot be recovered"; after SPEC-5f an idle-suspended session can also be in this paragraph (failed release checkpoint) and has no interrupt context. Not caused by the SPEC-5d2 deletion. EVIDENCE: spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:236, :230.

### [spec.2.fix-design-G3.1]
DECISION: The SPEC-1f item 1 (§4.2) pointer becomes a bare pointer ("See [§10.1.1] **Generation counters:**.") that names its target and nothing about its contents — BECAUSE the round-1 pointer described what §10.1.1 holds ("its baseline and the writes that advance it") and that description went false against the terminal bump in §7.2 snapshot-close step 4(a); a bare pointer cannot go stale — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's narrowed description ("its baseline, the takeover increment, and the resume increment") is the same claim enumerated, and fails again the next time §10.1.1 changes; adding the terminal bump to §10.1.1 creates a second home for a rule §7.2 step 4 owns and the kept remainder of the §4.2 bullet already summarizes.
WATCHOUT: Do not describe a pointer's target in SPEC-1f; the terminal bump's home is §7.2 snapshot-close step 4 "Generation bookkeeping." — EVIDENCE: spec/07_session-lifecycle.md (step 4 (a)), spec/04_system-components.md (§4.2 session-records bullet, kept text "mid-resume terminal collapses ... bump coordination_generation under the terminal write").

### [redesign.1.apply]
FACT: Applied every edit of the 0092 redesign 1 consolidated edit list (E1 to E58, including E14a, E16a, E21a, E29a to E29c, and E35a to E35c). Every anchor matched once, and no edit was skipped.
DECISION: Held-message custody is redesigned. SPEC-5i **Delivery after a commit to `running`.** and **Enqueue after a drain.** become **Delivery of held messages.** and **Enqueue racing a terminal transition.**; CODE-4 item 6 `deliverBuffered` peeks the older of the inbox and DLQ heads, removes an entry only after its `Send`, and runs at every commit to `running` and before every path-2 delivery; CODE-4 item 7 `holdMessage` removes its own entries only on a terminal or failed re-read; CODE-6 item 2.2 keys the backlog resume on `HeldLen` by suspension reason; CODE-6 item 12 sweeps `resuming` and skips `suspended`; SPEC-5z items 6 and 7 and CODE-4 item 18 delete the durable-inbox `EXPIRE` and score migrated entries at acceptance — BECAUSE the 2026-10-08 run stopped for redesign of held-message delivery: a stopped drain left entries in a `running` DLQ with no delivery point, and the retract-and-re-route raced the drain.
DECISION: The orphan-claim GC interaction is redesigned. SPEC-1b **Exits from `resuming`.**, CODE-4 item 5.3, summary decision 15, and the shutdown edge case name the §4.6.1 orphan-claim GC as the second releaser of a stopped attempt's pod and accept its drain of a restore older than `claimOrphanTimeout`; CODE-4 item 19 adds the `--max-resuming-seconds` help — BECAUSE the attempt was stated as the only releaser while the GC already reclaims the pod, and the two binaries cannot enforce an ordering of their timeouts.
DECISION: Deleted from the change files: `holdMessage`'s `retracted` result and its re-routing, `DLQ.PopOldest`, `DLQ.RemoveAll`, `DLQ.Restore`, the `SweepExpired` call in `deliverBuffered`, the DLQ sweep of `suspended` rows, the state-free `dlq_ttl_expired` text (old SPEC-5z item 1), `Coordinator.DLQLen`, RECORDS-1 finding 7(i) on the failed re-read with its summary defect bullet, and the superseded tests `TestDeliverBufferedDrainsInboxThenDLQInScoreOrder`, `TestDeliverBufferedStopsOnSendErrorAndRestoresFailedAndUnattempted`, `TestDeliverBufferedExpiresPastTTLEntries`, `TestHoldMessageRetractsWhenRowRunning`, `TestHoldMessageRetractsWhenRowTerminal`, `TestDeliverBufferedLeavesFailedInboxEntryAtHead`, `TestHoldMessageLeavesDrainedMessage`, `TestDLQPopOldestTakesEachEntryOnce`, `TestDLQRemoveAllIsAllOrNothing`, `TestDLQRestoreKeepsScore`, and `TestSweepDLQExpiryCoversSuspended`.
DECISION: Reconciled outside the edit list: summary decision 4 and decision 18, four summary watch-outs (DLQ score order, `errHeldDeliveryFailed`, delivery before path 2, and the two `EXPIRE` tests), the CODE-4 and CODE-6 deliverable-index lines, checklist S11 and S12, the CODE-6 targets (`dlq.go` for `CountAcceptedAfter`), and the remaining "drain" wording for held-message delivery in CODE-4 item 8, CODE-6 items 2.2 and 7, and the CODE-6 and TEST-1 tests.
OPEN: OD-R1 and OD-R2, recorded in `### Open` with their defaults.
WATCHOUT: Replaced: the drain after a commit to `running` with its stopped-drain restore and `PopOldest`/`RemoveAll`/`Restore`. It lost because a stopped drain left entries in a `running` DLQ that waited until the next commit.
WATCHOUT: Replaced: `holdMessage`'s retract-and-re-route on a `running` re-read. It lost because it raced the drain and needed an all-or-nothing removal script, while a level-triggered delivery before each path-2 message makes the race harmless.
WATCHOUT: Replaced: the DLQ sweep of `suspended` rows and the state-free `dlq_ttl_expired` definition. They lost because the sweep raced the backlog resume, and the state-free definition existed only to cover entries a stopped drain left behind.
WATCHOUT: Replaced: the durable-inbox `EXPIRE` on `resume_pending`. It lost because it deletes entries with no `message_expired`, including entries a `running` session buffered after the resume.

### [spec.1.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: The suspended-pod release guard identifies the suspension by its entry timestamp: SPEC-5b step 2 conditions the binding clear on "the same entry to `suspended` with that pod bound", and CODE-6 item 4.3 guards on `State == suspended && SuspendedAt.Equal(row.SuspendedAt) && PodAssignment == row.PodAssignment` — BECAUSE state plus pod is ABA-prone (resume on the held pod, a turn, a second interrupt restores both during the release's checkpoint, so the stale release frees the new suspension's pod early), and every entry to `suspended` restamps `SuspendedAt` — ALTERNATIVES: a suspension epoch column (new state `SuspendedAt` already carries); guarding on `coordination_generation` (a held-pod resume need not advance it, and redesign 1 reserved it for resume exclusivity); a lock across the checkpoint (per replica; CODE-6 item 4 takes no delivery lock).
WATCHOUT: Compare `SuspendedAt` with `time.Time.Equal`, never `==`; the row round-trips through pgstore timestamptz. Do not restore the state-plus-pod-only release guard. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/interrupt.go:87 (interrupt stamps `SuspendedAt = now`); CODE-6 item 5.3 `SuspendIdle` stamps it too; CODE-6 item 2.1's expiry write already guards on `SuspendedAt` unchanged.

### [spec.1.review-kubernetes.1]
FACT: Binder.Resume always claims a fresh idle pod through connect and then reserves a slot on that pod, so a resume's replacement pod serves no other session before the commit. SPEC-1b's "No session's `pod_assignment` names that pod" therefore holds on concurrent-workspace pools too, and the WPC orphan GC predicate 1 can select it. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/resume.go:128-146; spec/04_system-components.md:517
FACT: The staged spec edits add no CRD field write, no finalizer, no admission-webhook dependency, and no controller reconcile on a request path. SandboxClaim spec and status stay gateway-owned (spec/04_system-components.md §4.6.3 table), REG-COORDLEASE is a Redis lease rather than a Kubernetes Lease (spec/28_communication-channels.md:137), and every new state edge is a Postgres row write. The Kubernetes-idiom lens found nothing in round 1.
WATCHOUT: SPEC-5b reorders the suspension release to clear the session's pod binding before releasing the pod. Between those two writes the claim is still `bound` with an old transition time and no session references it, so a WPC GC tick can drain a pod the gateway is about to recycle. The outcome fails closed (a drain), and it is the GC lookup-then-drain race the Standing context already records as predating this proposal. Do not file it as a new defect. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:517

### [spec.1.review-mechanism.1]

FACT: The `error`/`inbox_unavailable` receipt reason is scoped in §15.4 to "Durable inbox enqueue failed because Redis was unreachable (`durableInbox: true` deployments only)"; SPEC-5i **Enqueue racing a terminal transition.** now emits it on a failed session re-read after a DLQ enqueue in any inbox mode, and no SPEC item edits the §15.4 row. EVIDENCE: spec/15_external-api-surface.md:1620, :1628; spec-changes.md SPEC-5i
FACT: Traced a message through idle suspension, zero-hold release, podless and held-pod resume, resume commit, and the level-triggered delivery on and off the coordinating replica; each lands in the DLQ or inbox and is delivered at a commit, a path-2 delivery, or after idle suspension plus backlog resume, or expires in a recovering state. No stranded-entry path found in the staged spec text beyond the two enum sites above.

Not filed. EVIDENCE: spec/10_gateway-internals.md §10.1.4 **Hold state timeout**
UNVERIFIED: An expiry sweep that listed a row as `resuming` (now swept per SPEC-5z) can remove and `message_expired` an entry the commit's held-message delivery just consumed. Not filed as speculative; a reliability lens could weigh it.

### [spec.1.review-performance.1]
USEFUL [spec.1.review-performance.1 WATCHOUT, 7-day suspended population]: it saved re-deriving and re-filing lease-renewal and backlog-probe scaling. For the record, the math is Tier 3 claim rate 30/s (spec/16_observability.md:609) × abandoned fraction f × 604800 s. That gives about 18M·f suspended rows, each with a REG-COORDLEASE renewal every 30 s and a DLQ ZCOUNT every 5 s watchdog tick. The family is still refuted as a design choice that no spec-stated population bound contradicts.
FACT: Redesign 3 puts a DLQ peek (Redis ZRANGE) in front of every path-2 delivery (SPEC-5i **Delivery of held messages.**; non-spec-changes.md CODE-4 item 6 "Callers"). A `Peek` error stops the loop (non-spec-changes.md:86). During a Redis outage, a path-2 message to a `running` session in default inbox mode is therefore queued in the inbox rather than delivered, until Redis returns. Not filed: it is the same degraded-mode family the redesign2 skeptic refuted (scratchpad/cp-log/0092-redesign2/history/spec.r2.history.md:25), no message is lost, and the shipped rate limiter already fails closed after 60 s of Redis outage (spec/12_storage-architecture.md:216, :232). A Redis lens that wants to reopen it needs a loss case or a spec contradiction, not a latency case.
FACT: The SPEC-5f admission-write failover finding was refuted in redesign2 (spec.r2.history.md:24). The skeptic's reason was that message paths already `store.Get` the row (messages.go:213), and that §12.3's "writes requiring Postgres ... are rejected" (spec/12_storage-architecture.md:153) covers it. Do not refile it.
DECISION: Returned no findings — BECAUSE every per-unit-of-work write the staged spec adds is either a Postgres row write already required per qualifying event (spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:265), the OD-A open cost, or a Redis op whose population scaling was refuted earlier. Postgres failover, Redis reset, and coordinator handoff leave `coordination_generation`, `retryCount`, and the DLQ entries no worse than shipped. Held messages are at-least-once across a handoff, and the duplicate is acknowledged in SPEC-5i — ALTERNATIVES: filing the head-of-line latency of delivering held messages before each path-2 message (rejected: it is redesign 3's owner-approved design, and a preference rather than a defect); filing the idle suspend/backlog-resume pod-restore loop for an undeliverable held entry (rejected: maxSessionAge active time bounds it to about 8 cycles, and no deterministic Send failure was shown).

USEFUL [review-log FACT at line 676]: its deliverable-to-edit map held through redesign 3 and saved the full re-enumeration.

### [spec.1.review-reliability.1]
FACT: The watchdog ticks every 5 s on every replica and sweepDLQExpiry runs each tick; with SPEC-5z it covers resume_pending, resuming, and awaiting_client_action. maxResumeWindowSeconds (the DLQ TTL, DefaultDLQTTL) and maxClientIdleSeconds both default to 900 s, so a held entry that reaches resume_pending through an idle suspension is almost always past its TTL before any restore commits. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go:166, :684-704; pkg/gateway/session/sessioninbox/dlq.go:22; spec/11_policy-and-controls.md:207
FACT: CODE-6 item 2 runs the backlog resume before the release in the same sweepSuspended tick, so the code resumes an idle-suspended session with held messages on its held pod. SPEC-5f ("releases ... at once") and SPEC-5i **Backlog resume.** do not state that order, and a release-first implementation routes those entries through resume_pending, where they expire. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 items 2-3, 5.3
CORRECTS [spec.1.fix-design-G3.1]: the FACT that enterResumePending makes a spec sentence about the coordinator's inbox unnecessary holds only when the coordinating replica writes resume_pending. An off-holder writer drains its own empty MemoryInbox ([spec.2.fix-design-G2.1] says so), and SPEC-5c deletes the §6.2 sentence that required the drain on suspended → resume_pending.
WATCHOUT: The SPEC-5b step 2 release guard (suspended plus the same pod) and CODE-6 item 4.3 can be fooled by ABA. Resume, then interrupt again during the release's checkpoint, and the old release passes the guard and cuts the new interrupt hold. Conditioning the guard on SuspendedAt closes it. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:400; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/interrupt.go:87

### [spec.2.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: Returned no findings in spec round 2 — BECAUSE tracing a message through idle suspension (zero hold, backlog check before release), the release racing an off-holder enqueue (level-triggered backlog resume picks the entry up on the next tick, podless), held-pod and podless resume, the resume commit, adoption after a coordinator crash, and the terminal race, each entry is delivered at least once with removal after delivery or leaves through TTL expiry in a recovering state, overflow, or the terminal drain. The round-1 fixes (SuspendedAt guard on the release, the TTL anchor sentence, the §4.7.1 fence row, the inbox_unavailable widening, the §7.2 enum-gloss edit) introduced no new recovery defect — ALTERNATIVES: filing the Redis-outage DLQ peek in front of path 2 (rejected: refuted in redesign 2 and recorded as not filed in [spec.1.review-performance.1]); filing the expiry-sweep-vs-commit-delivery race (rejected: at-least-once already admits it, and the window predates SPEC-5z); filing clock skew between EnqueuedAt and SuspendedAt in the interrupt backlog predicate (rejected: the spec states the predicate abstractly; the clock source is a code choice).
USEFUL [spec.1.review-performance.1 FACT, Redis-outage DLQ peek]: saved re-filing the path-2 Redis dependency.
USEFUL [review-log FACT line 489, resume_pending keeps PodAssignment]: settles what "no bound pod" means for the SPEC-2b fence skip.
FACT: Item 7 of SPEC-5z (migration scored at acceptance) makes default-mode migrated inbox entries older than the TTL expire at the first sweep in resume_pending. That matches durable mode, whose trimmer already measures from enqueued_at once the session is recovering. It is not a regression relative to durable mode. EVIDENCE: spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:302
UNVERIFIED: The backlog predicate for a non-Idle suspension compares a serving replica's EnqueuedAt with SuspendedAt, which another replica stamps. Skew of a few ms can leave an off-holder message to a held-pod interrupt suspension uncounted, so it waits for the client. A code-loop reviewer should check which clock SuspendedAt uses (non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 item 2.2).

### [spec.3.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: The receipt of a path-6 DLQ enqueue is stated once, in SPEC-5i **Held messages.** (queued/dropped) with **Enqueue racing a terminal transition.** (error/terminal answers, which already applies to every DLQ enqueue under the path). **Synchronous delivery.** and **Delivery of held messages.** point at **Held messages.** for the receipt instead of writing `queued` — BECAUSE the inline copies disagreed with the overflow, failed-enqueue and terminal answers — ALTERNATIVES: routing the synchronous fallback wholly through **Held messages.** (rejected: its resume request would have the coordinator retry the `suspended → running` write that just failed, while CODE-6 item for `ActionResumeAndDeliver` writes `enterResumePending` only when the fresh row names no pod and nothing otherwise); deleting the receipt words with no pointer (rejected: **Held messages.** covers only "every other message", so the synchronous fallback would have no stated receipt).
WATCHOUT: The synchronous-fallback clause "and **Backlog resume.** resumes the session" does not match the code deliverable, which writes `enterResumePending` at once when the fresh row names no pod (non-spec-changes CODE-6, REST `ActionResumeAndDeliver` bullet). Not in G1's scope; a later round may file it.
DECISION: The §15.4 `delivery` table's suspended exception is stated once, in the sentence SPEC-5k inserts below the table, which now overrides the whole row (custody and receipt); the `"queued"` row edit is deleted — BECAUSE an exception that covers only the receipt leaves the row's inbox custody standing — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's rewrite of the whole `"queued"` cell to lead with the exception (rejected: two statements of one exception, cell and sentence).
DECISION: §7.2 **Sibling coordination patterns:** item 1 gets a pointer keyed on the DLQ (SPEC-5i2 item 3), not on the `suspended` state — BECAUSE every DLQ entry (suspended and recovering) is enqueued by the serving replica and ordered by **Delivery of held messages.**, so a pointer keyed on the store states the class once rather than adding a per-state exception.
FACT: §15.4 **Ordering guarantee:** (spec/15_external-api-surface.md:1676, "coordinator-local sequence number assigned at inbox-enqueue time") was already false before this proposal for path-2 messages and recovering-state DLQ entries, neither of which is inbox-enqueued. Left as a separate finding.

### [spec.4.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: deleted the last sentence of SPEC-5i **Backlog resume.** ("A session suspended by an interrupt keeps its inbox entries ... held until the client acts ...") and changed the SPEC-5y step 9 replacement to "until a message or a resume call resumes it ([§7.2](07_session-lifecycle.md#72-interactive-session-model) delivery path 6), or the session ends" — BECAUSE path 6 resumes a `suspended` session on a message from any source (including another session's `lenny/send_message`), so "until the client acts" is false; the bullet's first-sentence predicate and **Delivery of held messages.** already govern which entries trigger a resume and that all are delivered after `running` — ALTERNATIVES: rewriting the sentence (second statement of path 6's rule inside path 6); keeping "client acts" plus lifetime clause (false); enumerating every end condition in step 9 (restates path 6 and §6.2).
WATCHOUT: an interrupt-suspended session does not wait for the client. Any message from any sender resumes it under path 6; only the Backlog resume sweep is restricted to DLQ entries accepted after `SuspendedAt`. Earlier settled log text saying such entries "wait for the client" (review-log Standing context, §29.4 step 9 rationale) describes the sweep's predicate, not a guarantee that only the client resumes the session. Do not add a sender-based condition to make "client acts" true. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-5i path 6 **Held messages.** / **Backlog resume.**; spec/29_communication-scenarios.md §29.4 step 9 ("until the client acts").
FACT: §29.4 step 9 base text wraps "until the client acts" at the end of a line before "([§6.2](...))"; SPEC-5y says keep wrapping. — EVIDENCE: spec/29_communication-scenarios.md §29.4 step 9.

### [spec.4.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: The last sentence of SPEC-5i **Backlog resume.** ("A session suspended by an interrupt keeps its inbox entries ... held until the client acts or the suspended-session lifetime expires the session") is deleted, and SPEC-5y's §29.4 step 9 replacement text becomes "until a message or a resume call resumes it ([§7.2] delivery path 6), or the session ends" — BECAUSE the Backlog resume predicate (sentence 1) already says which entries trigger a backlog resume, **Delivery of held messages.** already delivers every held entry once the session is `running`, and path 6 opening plus **Held messages.** resume a `suspended` session on any message from any sender; the deleted sentence only restated a consequence with a wrong end condition — ALTERNATIVES: reviewer's replace-in-place of the Backlog sentence (keeps a restatement of path 6's own resume rule inside path 6); keeping "the client acts" plus a lifetime clause (false: a parent's `lenny/send_message` resumes it).
WATCHOUT: The reason non-Idle suspensions do not count pre-suspension entries (interrupt pause-and-decide) lives in review-log Settled decision at review-log.md:16, not in staged text. That entry's phrase "wait for the client ... until the client acts" is history; read it as "do not by themselves trigger a backlog resume". EVIDENCE: review-log.md:16; spec-changes.md:483-488.
FACT: In the base spec, §29.4 step 9 is the only "until the client acts" in spec/29; spec/06:290's occurrence is replaced wholesale by SPEC-5g. EVIDENCE: spec/29_communication-scenarios.md:690.

### [spec.4.review-mechanism.1]
FACT: The code keeps `PodAssignment` on a row that enters `resume_pending` (failure.go:337-338, :379-380, :424-425 copy the reporter's pod in), so a recovering row can name a dead pod. SPEC-2b's "no bound pod" therefore has to be read from state as well as `pod_assignment`; CODE-5's predicate treats `resume_pending` and `awaiting_client_action` as podless whatever the column holds (non-spec-changes.md:272). Do not refile SPEC-2b as "fences a dead pod" without checking that reading. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/failure.go:337
DECISION: Did not file the SPEC-5f clause "a delivery refused after that write ... routes it by §7.2 delivery path 6" as a conflict with the **Delivery of held messages.** failure rule ("the entry stays at the head of its queue") for a refused held entry — BECAUSE the worst reading re-enqueues a copy, and a duplicate is inside the at-least-once guarantee, so nothing becomes inconsistent or broken. CODE-6 item 7 re-routes only the new batch, and `deliverBuffered` leaves the held entry where it is (non-spec-changes.md:86, :155).
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md]: CODE-6 item 7 (`ResumeSuspended`, line 158) says that when the row names a pod and the lease gate refuses this replica, "the coordinating replica's backlog resume (item 2) resumes the session". A backlog resume fires only on held messages, and for an interrupt suspension only on DLQ entries accepted after `SuspendedAt`. If any caller sends a message-less `resume_session` there, the claim is false and the call is silently dropped. The fix: fail closed as the REST `/resume` held-pod off-holder branch does (TARGET_NOT_READY, CODE-4 item 8), or confirm that only message paths call `ResumeSuspended` and say so.

### [spec.7.review-kubernetes.1]
FACT: The staged spec edits touch no CRD field, status subresource, finalizer, webhook, or controller reconcile. REG-COORDLEASE is a Redis compare-and-set key (spec/28_communication-channels.md:137), not a Kubernetes Lease, and every resume or suspend wait runs off the request path (POST /resume answers 200 with resume_pending). The only Kubernetes surface is the WPC orphan-claim GC that SPEC-1b cites, and it matches §4.6.1 predicate 1 — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:517; pkg/controller/warmpool/gc.go:79-84 (PodHasActiveSession = any non-terminal session bound to the pod).

Not filed — EVIDENCE: spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:229.

### [spec.7.review-performance.1]

DECISION: Returned no findings in spec round 7 — BECAUSE the only change since the r5 snapshot is the SPEC-1b **Retry accounting.** sentence ("No other entry into `resume_pending` advances it."), which adds no write and no store dependency. Tracing a message through idle suspension (backlog check precedes the zero-hold release in the same tick), the off-holder hold into a held-pod session (next coordinator tick), podless and held-pod resume, and the resume commit gives at-least-once with removal after delivery, or TTL expiry once in a recovering state — ALTERNATIVES: re-filing the 7-day suspended-population lease and backlog-probe scaling (refuted family; review-log WATCHOUT at :670); the Redis-outage DLQ peek in front of path 2 (not-filed family; review-log :871, :971); the admission write as a Postgres dependency (refuted, redesign2 spec.r2.history.md:24).
FACT: §12.4 Tier 3 lease-renewal row is "~1,000/s ... one per coordination TTL / 2 — the only row that scales directly with concurrent session count", and Tier 3 creation rate there is 200/s sustained. EVIDENCE: spec/12_storage-architecture.md:258, :266
FACT: A held-pod `suspended` pod failure takes the standard retry path and restores to `running`, not `suspended` (spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:236). For an idle suspension the window is one coordinator tick, because SPEC-5b step 2 clears the binding before the release; not a regression. EVIDENCE: spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:236; spec-changes.md SPEC-5b step 2
USEFUL [spec.1.review-performance.1 WATCHOUT, 7-day suspended population]: saved re-deriving and re-filing the lease and backlog-probe scaling a third time.

### [spec.7.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: Returned no findings under the reliability lens for round 7 — BECAUSE every traced message edge either delivers at least once with removal after `Send` or expires through the recovering-state sweep. The edges traced were idle suspension with zero-hold release racing a coordinator-held message, podless and held-pod resume on and off the coordinator, a coordinator crash after a `running` commit and before held delivery (picked up by the next path-2 delivery or by idle suspension plus **Backlog resume.**), a serving-replica crash between DLQ enqueue and the state write (**Backlog resume.**), and an enqueue racing a terminal transition. Driver crash before the claim, before entry, and after the claim end in the `resuming` watchdog or the §4.6.1 predicate-1 claim GC. The coordination_generation progression has one home (§10.1.1 via SPEC-1f item 2) — ALTERNATIVES: refiling Redis- or Postgres-outage stalls of path-2 delivery (the DLQ peek and the admission write). The redesign2 history refuted both (spec.r2.history.md, the admission-write and DLQ-custody entries), and review-log FACT at :871 records the peek case.
FACT: Partial-manifest selection on resume uses MAX(coordination_generation) over the session's manifests (spec/10_gateway-internals.md:171). The entry increment in SPEC-1b leaves it intact because no manifest is written during `resuming` and every later checkpoint is stamped with a generation at least as high — EVIDENCE: spec/10_gateway-internals.md:153,157,171
USEFUL [review-log Standing context, the DLQ-custody and level-triggered-delivery DECISIONs]: these let the trace skip the retired drain-after-commit variants.

### [spec.7.review-security.1]
FACT: The SPEC-5b release disposition (recycle on a recycling pool, drain otherwise, slot-only on a concurrent pod) tightens the current §6.2 step 2 "releases the pod back to the pool"; it does not regress one-session-only reuse, and the §5.2 tenant pin read still gates every resume-driver claim (SPEC-3h makes a failed pin read claim nothing). EVIDENCE: spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:229; spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:448
FACT: Before this proposal, any inter-session `lenny/send_message` carrying `delivery: "immediate"` already resumed an interrupt-suspended session, so "any message resumes" widens an existing route and does not bypass the interrupt pause control. EVIDENCE: spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:333-337
FACT: Skipping the §10.1.2 step 2 fence for a podless session (SPEC-2b) leaves no unfenced pod. The replacement pod is known only to the attempt that claimed it until the commit, and CODE-4 item 5.4 calls `fenceResumedPod` after the commit. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:79; spec/10_gateway-internals.md:41 (step 3, "no fenced generation" case)
FACT: The delegation budget TTL bound fails closed on expiry (the sentinel check returns BUDGET_KEYS_EXPIRED, and the root moves to failed), so the SPEC-5o re-arm cannot silently reset a budget ceiling. EVIDENCE: spec/08_recursive-delegation.md:408-414
UNVERIFIED: The failure edges stamp the failed pod's name onto `PodAssignment` for `resume_pending` (failure.go:337-338, :379-380, :424-425). SPEC-2b keys the fence skip on "no bound pod", and CODE-5 tests treat `resume_pending` as podless by state (non-spec-changes.md:272). Whether "bound pod" means a non-null `pod_assignment` matters for liveness. The reliability or mechanism lens should check it; it is not a security regression.
OPEN: The Redis DLQ key (`t:{tenant_id}:session:{session_id}:dlq`) carries no key-level EXPIRE and is not in the §12.8 erasure scope table (only `session_dlq_archive` is). Under SPEC-5i, entries can stay held for up to 7 days in `suspended`. This gap predates the proposal and is a compliance/retention matter, so it was not filed. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessioninbox/dlq.go:87-95; spec/12_storage-architecture.md:825

### [spec.9.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: SPEC-5f **Idle suspension.** no longer gives an outcome for a refused held-message delivery; it points to §7.2 **Delivery of held messages.** (SPEC-5i), which is the one home for a failed held delivery (entry stays at its queue head), and routes any other refused message again by the session's then-current state — BECAUSE a held delivery is covered by the admission write and SuspendIdle runs without the delivery lock, so the old "route it by path 6" re-enqueued a held entry (duplicate inbox copy, re-scored DLQ entry) — ALTERNATIVES: reviewer's "held stays held, other by path 6" (hard-coded path 6 is wrong once a resume commits `running` before the re-route, and diverges from **Synchronous delivery.** and CODE-6 item 7); deleting the clause (refused non-held path-2 message would have no stated outcome).
WATCHOUT: Never route a refused held entry (inbox or DLQ) by path 6 or re-enqueue it; that duplicates inbox entries and breaks DLQ acceptance order. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-5i **Delivery of held messages.** "the entry stays at the head of its queue"

### [spec.9.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: SPEC-5f **Idle suspension.** stops stating where a refused held message goes; it says a refused held message stays held, citing **Delivery of held messages.** (SPEC-5i) as the home, and routes any other refused message again by the state the session is then in. BECAUSE the failure outcome of a held delivery already has one home (SPEC-5i "When the delivery of a held message fails, the replica stops and the entry stays at the head of its queue"), and "route by the state then in" is what CODE-6 item 7 builds (re-classify the fresh row) and what **Synchronous delivery.** already says. ALTERNATIVES: deleting the SPEC-5f clause outright leaves a refused non-held path-2 message (no held entries ahead of it) with no stated outcome, because path 2 itself only defines the timeout fallthrough to path 5; keeping "routes it by path 6" for non-held messages is wrong if a resume wrote `running` before the re-route.
FACT: a path-2 Send cannot be refused after a successful held delivery in the same pass, because the held delivery's StampSync advances last_agent_activity_at and the suspension write is guarded on it unchanged; the only refused new-message case is when no held entry was delivered first. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-5f **Idle suspension.**; non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 item 5.1 StampSync.
WATCHOUT: summary item 16 says "a refused batch is routed by §7.2 delivery path 6"; "batch" there is the new message, so it stays true and was left alone. Do not extend it to held entries.

CODE-6 item 7 re-routes only the new batch, so the code follows path 6. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:443, :488; non-spec-changes.md:155.

### [spec.9.review-performance.1]
DECISION: Returned no findings in spec round 9 — BECAUSE the only changes since the r7 snapshot are the SPEC-1f item 2 baseline clause ("no takeover and no resume driver write has advanced") and the SPEC-5i2 item 4 §15.4 ordering pointer, and neither adds a store write, a watch, or a store dependency. Both anchors resolve (spec/15_external-api-surface.md:1676 "coordinator-local FIFO — not global wall-clock order"; spec/10_gateway-internals.md:30) — ALTERNATIVES: re-filing the 7-day suspended-population lease and backlog-probe scaling (refuted family, review-log WATCHOUT at :670); the per-Send admission write (OD-A open).
FACT: The watchdog tick is 5 s (pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go:166), and the coordination Sweeper lists every non-terminal row per tenant and upserts the REG-COORDMIRROR row for every held lease on each 15 s sweep (pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:303, :491). So every podless `suspended` row that SPEC-2a keeps under lease costs one Postgres mirror upsert per sweep, in addition to its Redis renewal. This belongs to the refuted population family, and the note is kept for a code-loop reviewer. EVIDENCE: as cited.

### [spec.11.review-reliability.1]
FACT: The serving replica stamps a held message's `EnqueuedAt` after it reads the session state, not when the request arrives (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/messages_inbox.go:48; pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcptools/mcptools_register.go:371). Interrupt `SuspendedAt` comes from the suspending replica's clock (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/interrupt.go:87). The SPEC-5i **Backlog resume.** "accepted after the session entered `suspended`" predicate is therefore exposed only to clock skew between replicas. EVIDENCE: the cited lines.
WATCHOUT: A message that an off-holder replica holds for a held-pod interrupt suspension, inside the inter-replica clock-skew window after the suspension write, is not counted by the backlog resume. It waits for the client or the next message. Nothing is lost, so this was not filed. Do not refile it as message loss. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-5i **Backlog resume.**
FACT: A `resume_pending` or `awaiting_client_action` row keeps the failed pod's name in `PodAssignment` (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/failure.go:337-338, :379-380; finalize.go:218 "a recovery-state row carrying a stale"). SPEC-2b's "no bound pod" therefore has to be read from the state as well as the column. CODE-5 already does this (non-spec-changes.md:272), so it was not filed.
USEFUL [Standing context, held-message delivery DECISION]: tracing idle suspension, zero-hold release, podless and held-pod resume, and resume commit on both the coordinating replica and an off-holder found no at-least-once gap. Each of these edges either reaches a delivery point (a commit, a path-2 message, or the idle suspension followed by the backlog resume) or ends in expiry or a terminal drain with `message_expired`.

### [spec.11.review-security.1]
FACT: The SPEC-5b release disposition ("recycling pool recycles, any other pod drains"; slot release on maxConcurrentSessions > 1) runs through the §5.2 Scrub model, which runs a per-slot cleanup on every session release and a whole-pod scrub at occupancy zero, and tenant pinning holds the recycled pod in `reserved` for its tenant. A mid-session release adds no unscrubbed reuse path. EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:462, :480, :588
FACT: Budget-key expiry fails closed (sentinel check returns BUDGET_KEYS_EXPIRED; the root goes to `failed`), so the SPEC-5o TTL rule cannot reset a budget. EVIDENCE: spec/08_recursive-delegation.md:408-414
FACT: Today's path 6 already lets a `lenny/send_message` with `delivery: "immediate"` resume an interrupt-suspended session, so SPEC-5i's "any message resumes" gives a peer session no new ability to undo a client interrupt. EVIDENCE: spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:333-337
WATCHOUT: In SPEC-2b, skipping the fence for a podless adopter stays safe for split-brain because the only pod-binding write (the driver's commit) is conditioned on the incarnation's coordination_generation, which the adopter's step 1 CAS advances. Do not refile the fence skip as a removed mandatory gate unless a pod-binding write that the generation does not condition appears.

### [non-spec.1.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: CODE-3 `Bind` hook-error release is `podclaim.DeleteClaim` alone — BECAUSE `ReclaimClaimed` calls `releaseCredentials(sessionID)` → `ReleaseSession`, which releases every lease of the session including a winning attempt's; the hook runs before `Prepare`, so this attempt has minted no lease — ALTERNATIVES: release by lease ID (nothing to release at that point).
WATCHOUT: `ReclaimClaimed` is the wrong release on any path that can run while another attempt for the same session holds leases (snapshotless rebuild via `startOnPod` → `Bind`). Do not restore it on the `Bind` hook path. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/reclaim.go:76-80; credentiallease.go:28-33
DECISION: The per-pool-kind release lives in one helper: CODE-3 changes `releaseResumeSlot` so an empty slot ID deletes the claim; on a slot pool it releases the reservation alone. The `Resume` hook-error path and `failResume` (CODE-4 item 5.3) both reach it — BECAUSE an unconditional `DeleteClaim` on a slot pool deletes the shared per-pod claim under a sibling that joined through ClaimSlot Pass 1, and today `failResume` never deletes an exclusive pool's claim (`releaseResumeSlot` returns nil on empty slot ID), leaving it for orphan GC — ALTERNATIVES: a per-site exclusive/slot conditional in the Resume bullet and again in item 5.3 (rejected: predicate stated twice).
WATCHOUT: Previously staged text "`releaseResumeSlot(leaked=false)`, and `podclaim.DeleteClaim`" on the Resume hook path drained a slot pod under a bound sibling. Do not add an explicit `DeleteClaim` next to `releaseResumeSlot` or after `failResume`. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/slotclaimer.go:858-899; podsession/resume.go:274-300
FACT: On a slot pool the occupancy-zero edge of `ReleaseSlotReservation` deletes the claim on a non-recycling pool and patches it to `recycling` on a recycling pool, so the staged text says the edge "disposes of" the claim rather than "deletes" it. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/slotclaimer.go:859-899
OPEN: CODE-4 item 5.3 names only `failResume`, which exists on the checkpoint branch; the snapshotless rebuild branch's `BindResult` (from `Bind`/`BindSlot`, holding leases this attempt minted) has no named commit-guard release, and `Binder.Release`/`rollbackBinding` revoke session-wide leases. Left for a later round per the design's siteDispositions.

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: Close the Resume hook-error release (finding "slot-pool Resume deletes the shared SandboxClaim") and CODE-4 item 5.3 (failResume does not delete the claim) with ONE helper change: `releaseResumeSlot` deletes the claim with `podclaim.DeleteClaim` when the slot ID is empty, and keeps the reservation release on a slot pool (whose occupancy-zero edge deletes the claim). The Resume hook-error release becomes `cl.Close` + `releaseResumeSlot(leaked=false)`; item 5.3 cites `failResume`, which already calls it after the pod-side reclaim — BECAUSE the per-pool-kind rule then has one home (the helper) and both callers inherit it — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewers' per-site conditional ("DeleteClaim on exclusive, releaseResumeSlot on slot") written into both the CODE-3 Resume bullet and item 5.3 (rejected: the same predicate stated twice, and an implementor of 5.3 still adds a DeleteClaim beside failResume); calling `Binder.Release`/`rollbackBinding` for 5.3 (rejected: release.go:66 `releaseCredentials(result.SessionID)` revokes every session lease, the scope defect of finding 1).
FACT: `failResume` on an exclusive pool today leaves the claim in place: `releaseResumeSlot` returns nil for an empty slot ID and nothing in `accountResumeSlotFailure` (slot_bind.go) deletes it, so a failed adapter Resume on an exclusive pool leaks the claim to orphan GC. The helper change fixes that path too. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/resume.go:274-297; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/slot_bind.go:452-457
FACT: `Binder.Bind` = `Claim` → `Prepare` → `Launch`; credentials are assigned inside `Prepare`, so a hook between `Claim` and `Prepare` follows no lease this attempt minted, and `ReclaimClaimed` (reclaim.go:76-80) only reaches leases another attempt or the original bind minted. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/binder.go:222-236; credentiallease.go:23-33
WATCHOUT: `ReleaseSlotReservation` (slotbinder.go:728) calls `SlotClaimer.ReleaseSlot` with recycle=false, so its occupancy-zero edge DELETEs the claim and never patches `recycling`; a sibling slot (ClaimSlot Pass 1 joining between the claim CREATE and the hook) keeps the claim. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/slotclaimer.go:858-899
OPEN: CODE-4 item 5.3 names only `failResume`, which is `Binder.Resume`-specific (it compensates the Resume's bindAttempt). On the snapshotless rebuild branch the `BindResult` comes from `Binder.Bind`/`BindSlot`, which minted leases and started a session; item 5.3 names no release for it, and the obvious `rollbackBinding`/`Binder.Release` revokes session-wide leases (release.go:66). Separate finding for a later round; not fixed here.

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: The binderless gateway is handled by one predicate `holdsPod(row)` (`PodAssignment != ""` or no pod binder wired), defined in CODE-6 item 6 and named by every caller that today says "names a pod" / "non-empty `PodAssignment`" (CODE-4 items 8 and 9, CODE-6 item 7 REST and `ResumeSuspended`); `DriveResume` also returns at once with no binder — BECAUSE without `--agent-namespace` no binder exists (cmd/lenny-gateway/pod_lifecycle.go:146), every suspended row is podless, and `resumeOnPod` dereferences `s.podBinder.Client` (resume_rebind.go:80) — ALTERNATIVES: a no-binder branch inside `attemptResume` that commits without a claim (a second resume code path); per-site "or no binder" clauses (hair: five copies of one predicate).
FACT: A binderless gateway produces no `resume_pending` or `awaiting_client_action` row of its own: the only `ReportSessionFailure` caller is the stream-failure path (stream_failure.go:68), which needs a pod binding. The `DriveResume` no-binder return is a nil-deref guard for seeded rows, not a stall of a reachable state. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/failure.go:126.
WATCHOUT: Staged SPEC-5i path 6 says a podless `suspended` session enters `resume_pending`. A binderless gateway (dev posture, no pod at all) resumes in place on its in-process executor. That is a tree posture the spec does not define (resume_held_pod.go doc comment); do not add a binder clause to the staged spec.
DECISION: PreMessageDelivery runs at acceptance: the existing MCP `PhasePreMessageDelivery` block (cooldown, `maxInputSize`, interceptor chain) moves ahead of `Classify` in mcptools_register.go, so a held or inbox entry is already scanned, and `deliverBuffered` runs no interceptor — BECAUSE today the buffer branch returns before the block (mcptools_register.go:356-394) and §4.8 scopes the phase to `lenny/send_message`, so REST needs nothing — ALTERNATIVES: scan inside `deliverBuffered` (sessionserver has no interceptor deps, a REJECT there has no caller to answer, and a held entry would be scanned against a policy that changed after acceptance).
DECISION: `deliverBuffered` stamps `From` from the entry's `SenderSessionID` through `senderFrom`, moved from mcptools.go:999 into the executor package, rather than a copy in sessionserver.
FACT: Existing tests that pin the synchronous `POST /resume` and its tail: resume_rebind_test.go (`TestResumeReleasesEarlierBindingBeforeBind_spec_10_1` :141, `TestResumeAgainstForeignLeaseHolderHoldsRow_spec_15_1` :197), resumed_event_test.go (incl. `TestResumeEmitsResumedBeforeChildrenReattached` :173), resume_metrics_audit_test.go, binding_generation_test.go:115-131 (resume subtests), resume_external_effect_regression_test.go:62, tests/tier4_integration/stream_failure_report_test.go:545, tests/tier5_e2e_kind/checkpoint_resume_test.go:142 (waits for awaiting_client_action then calls Resume), tests/tier7a_load_local/failure_funnel_race_test.go:259 (races report with POST /resume). There is no `sessiondriver` package; the finding's "sessiondriver.Resume doc comment" is CODE-4 item 13's SDK comment.

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G3.1]
DECISION: `ReleaseSuspendedPod` runs off the serial Tick on a goroutine with its own per-replica in-flight set, pointing at the CODE-6 item 2.2 `ResumeBacklog` pattern, and its checkpoint context carries the §4.4 **Checkpoint timeout and recovery:** 60 s deadline, a deadline counting as a checkpoint failure that takes the existing 60 s retry — BECAUSE production wires `checkpointer.Checkpointer` with `Deadline` zero, so the checkpoint stream is unbounded on the gateway side, and `Watchdog.Run` calls `Tick` serially — ALTERNATIVES: sharing the backlog resume's in-flight set (rejected: a release's checkpoint, up to 60 s, would then defer a backlog resume, whereas run concurrently the resume wins through the row guards and step 3 releases nothing); one shared go-once helper across `DriveResume`, `ResumeBacklog`, and the release (more coherent, but it rewrites CODE-4 item 2 and item 2.2, so it is a refactor for a later round); setting `Checkpointer.Deadline` in `pod_lifecycle.go` (it also changes periodic checkpoints, which belongs to a separate finding).
FACT: The production checkpointer has no gateway-side deadline. `Checkpointer.Deadline` "Zero lets the adapter apply its own §4.4 default", and cmd/lenny-gateway does not set it. §4.4 says "All checkpoint paths enforce a 60-second checkpoint timeout". EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/checkpoint/checkpointer/checkpointer.go:104-106, :510-516; cmd/lenny-gateway/pod_lifecycle.go:349-370; spec/04_system-components.md:261.
OPEN: The periodic and drain checkpoint paths run with no gateway-side 60 s deadline in production (Deadline unset). This is a §4.4 fidelity finding against the checkpoint area and is not owned by 0092.
WATCHOUT: The watchdog test "releases an `Idle` row on the first tick" stays correct after the release goes asynchronous, because the asynchrony is inside the session-server hook and the watchdog still calls the hook on the first tick. Do not rewrite that watchdog test to wait for a release.
DECISION: The idle-clock stamp test asserts only `LastAgentActivityAt == now` after `resumeHeldPod` and after the CODE-4 commit — BECAUSE that equality is the assertion that discriminates; `sweepIdle`'s anchor on `LastAgentActivityAt` is existing code (watchdog.go:1013-1019), so a cross-package follow-on tick adds no discrimination.

### [non-spec.1.review-kubernetes.1]
FACT: On a pool with maxConcurrentSessions > 1, Binder.Resume claims an idle pod through the exclusive Claimer (claim CREATE, bound) and only then reserves the slot; ClaimSlot Pass 1 lets any same-tenant start join a non-terminal per-pod claim in between, so the replacement pod can host sibling sessions before the resume's hook runs — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/resume.go:128-146, :231-235; pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/slotclaimer.go:411-470
FACT: podclaim.DeleteClaim deletes claim-<pod> by name with no occupancy check; on a slot pool the claim is deleted only by SlotClaimer.ReleaseSlot when the Redis counter reaches zero — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/claimer.go:433-443; slotclaimer.go:858-899
FACT: failResume does not delete the claim on an exclusive pool; it compensates, closes, and releases the slot reservation (which deletes the claim only at slot-pool occupancy zero) — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/resume.go:274-283, :291-300
WATCHOUT: Any staged release of a resume's replacement pod must dispatch on SlotID: DeleteClaim only on an exclusive pool, releaseResumeSlot alone on a slot pool. CODE-3 item 3 (Resume) and CODE-4 item 5.3 both state an unconditional claim deletion — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:53, :78
FACT: The lens found no status-as-inbox, finalizer, webhook, or controller-on-hot-path issue: the proposal adds no CRD writes beyond existing SandboxClaim create/delete/status by the gateway and relies on the WPC orphan GC as already specified (§4.6.1 predicate 1, §4.6.3 table).

### [non-spec.1.review-performance.1]
DECISION: Returned no findings in non-spec round 1 — BECAUSE the only changes since the r9 snapshot are checklist wording (S1-S5 labels, S? "Depends on: S1, S2"), the summary's restored open-decision list and deliverable-index wording, and one SPEC-5f sentence; none adds a store write, a watch, a per-row Redis call, or a store dependency. Traced the non-spec staging for top-tier cost and failure modes: StampSync per Send (one row UPDATE per batch; pgstore.Update always issues the UPDATE, pgstore.go:420-460) is smaller than the existing per-event AdvanceLastSeq row write; DriveResume's lease Get runs per resume_pending row (window-bounded population); ResumeBacklog/ResumeSuspended run off the Tick goroutine (CODE-6 item 2.2) — ALTERNATIVES: re-filing the sweepSuspended HeldLen probe (a Redis ZCOUNT per suspended row per replica per 5 s tick, run before the lease gate) against the 7-day population (refuted family, review-log WATCHOUT :670); an ambiguous Postgres COMMIT in completeResume, whose store-error branch CODE-4 item 5 leaves unstated beyond "when the guard fails" (not filed: rare, recoverable through the resuming watchdog and the orphan GC when the attempt simply stops; an implementor who releases on every Update error would leave a running row naming a released pod, so a later lens may want to check how the landed code handles it); mass-resume claim bursts after node loss (spec §7.3 already requires automatic retry, so not new to this proposal).
FACT: The watchdog Tick runs every sweep serially per tenant on every replica (pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go:588-680), and sweepMaxAge and the coordination Sweeper both list with an empty ListFilter, i.e. every row including terminal ones (watchdog.go:1075; coordination.go:303). Per-row listing cost over the suspended population is therefore pre-existing and not new to this proposal. EVIDENCE: as cited.
UNVERIFIED: completeResume's handling of a store error on the guarded commit Update that is not a guard miss (Postgres failover, lost COMMIT ack). CODE-4 item 5.3 states only the guard-miss branch. The implementor or the proposal-conformance pass should confirm the landed code does not release the pod on an ambiguous commit error without re-reading the row.
USEFUL [spec.1.review-performance.1 WATCHOUT, 7-day suspended population]: saved re-filing the HeldLen probe and lease scaling a sixth time.

### [non-spec.2.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: A lost commit releases its pod through a new `Binder.ReleaseUncommitted` (CODE-4 item 5.3): `ReleaseSlot` on a slot pool, `Release(result, "failed")` otherwise, with `releaseCredentials` factored out so it is skipped — BECAUSE every existing release that fits both branches revokes every lease of the session through `Credentials.ReleaseSession(sessionID)` — ALTERNATIVES: `failResume` (needs the Resume-local `bindAttempt` token no result carries, and uses the resume-reservation ledger that is wrong for the rebuild branch's Bind/BindSlot result); `rollbackBinding`/`Release`/`ReleaseSlot`/`ReclaimClaimed` (each calls `releaseCredentials`); a keep-credentials flag on `Release` (a dual mode).
WATCHOUT: Do not restore `failResume` or name `rollbackBinding` for the lost-commit release. `failResume` sends a reclaim keyed on `bindAttempt` (podsession/resume.go:151, :274), which the adapter answers SUPERSEDED for a foreign token (pkg/adapter/session.go:392-396, rule 13); `rollbackBinding` revokes the leases of a winning later attempt. EVIDENCE: sessionserver/bind_rollback.go:20-33; podsession/release.go:66; slotbinder.go:783; credentiallease.go:139-143.
FACT: `ReleaseSlot` calls `releaseCredentials` after the adapter Shutdown (slotbinder.go:775-783), while `Release` calls it first (release.go:66). The staged refactor moves it first in `ReleaseSlot` too; credential release and adapter teardown are independent, so the reorder is behaviour-neutral. EVIDENCE: podlifecycle/podsession/slotbinder.go:762-790.
DECISION: The rebuild branch publishes through `publishBinding`, and `resumeOnPod` no longer acquires the lease on its checkpoint branch (CODE-4 items 3.3, 4, 5.4); item 3.3's acquire is the one acquire — BECAUSE the tree forbids gating a post-commit publish on a second acquire (sessionserver/bind_registration.go:43-55), and the checkpoint-branch acquire's error path calls `rollbackBinding` (resume_rebind.go:117-118), which revokes session-wide leases — ALTERNATIVES: roll back the commit on a `registerBinding` error (the row would name a released pod); retry the acquire (unneeded).
CORRECTS [review-log.md:1474 "item 5.3 cites `failResume`"]: item 5.3 now names `ReleaseUncommitted`; the review-log OPEN at :1434/:1478 about `failResume`'s token is closed by it.

Do not add an acquire inside either function. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/resume_rebind.go releaseEarlierBinding doc comment.
WATCHOUT: `TestResumeReleasesEarlierBindingBeforeBind_spec_10_1` asserts `[sbx-old:failed]` before the new bind; that is the assertion that discriminates and it holds only because the driver goes through releaseThenResumeOnPod. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/resume_rebind_test.go.
DECISION: spec-map sentence in the last "Tests that land with CODE-4" bullet is the rule "Remove every deleted test's entries ... add the replacing driver tests" — BECAUSE the closed list "both deleted tests" missed the TestHoldOrFailOnResumeError* entries, and the TEST-1 tier-4 non-holder case lands at S14 with its own mapping, so S11 must not map it — ALTERNATIVES: move the tier-4 case into CODE-4 (against the Testing lead split); enumerate names (closed list again).
FACT: resume_setup_demotion_internal_test.go holds TestHoldOrFailOnResumeError{SetupCommand,SlotRefusals,KeepsNewerState}_spec_7_3 (call the deleted method, now deleted) plus TestIsTransientPodClaimErrorIgnoresPlainFailedPrecondition_spec_7_3 and TestResumeFailureReachesTheSlotAccounting_spec_5_2 (do not call it, survive). EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/resume_setup_demotion_internal_test.go.
OPEN: after holdOrFailOnResumeError is deleted, `isTransientPodClaimError` and `errResumeHoldStale` have no production caller; CODE-4 items 8 and 10 do not dispose of them (marked separate-finding by the G2 design).

### [non-spec.2.fix-G3.1]
DECISION: A PostAgentOutput REJECT on a held message records the §16.7 audit row, publishes and transcribes nothing for the entry, removes it, and continues; an audit-write failure stops the loop leaving the entry at the head (CODE-4 item 6) — BECAUSE the gate's verdict is final once the message reached the runtime, and leaving the entry wedges every later path-2 delivery — ALTERNATIVES: a new rejection event on the stream (new protocol surface, the audit row already records it); treating REJECT as a delivery failure (head-of-line block); a MODIFY test (already pinned by TestRunPostAgentOutputModifyRewritesParts).
WATCHOUT: `recordRouteRejection` calls `writeError(w, ...)` unconditionally, including on audit failure, and always returns false, so passing a nil writer panics and the caller cannot tell an audit failure from a REJECT. The refactor moves the envelope write to the REST callers and must surface the audit error separately. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/route.go:117-160, :169-186; messages_delivery.go:309-330.
FACT: `recordDeliveredResponse` takes `r *http.Request` only for its context; on REJECT today it transcribes and publishes nothing, inbound messages included. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/messages_delivery.go:309-345.

### [non-spec.2.fix-G6.1]
FACT: Delegation budget counter keys get a hard-coded 24 h TTL (`treebudget.DefaultTTL`) from the reserve Lua script only when `TTL == -1`, so a key's TTL is set on creation and never re-armed; the token-grant key is refreshed on each grant.
