### [non-spec.2.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: The lost-commit release (CODE-4 item 5.3) is a new `Binder.ReleaseUncommitted(ctx, *BindResult)`: `Release(result, "failed")` on an exclusive pool and `ReleaseSlot` on a slot pool, without `releaseCredentials`, by factoring the credential release out of both — BECAUSE every existing post-RPC release revokes every lease of the session (`ReleaseSession`), which on a lost commit whose session continues (watchdog exit to `resume_pending`, then a re-entry at G+1) strips the leases the original bind or the winning attempt holds — ALTERNATIVES: `failResume` (needs the Resume's local `bindAttempt`, which no result carries, so the reclaim is answered SUPERSEDED; and `releaseResumeSlot` is the wrong ledger for the rebuild branch's Bind/BindSlot result); `rollbackBinding` (the finding's suggestion; Release and ReleaseSlot both call `releaseCredentials` → session-wide `ReleaseSession`, the defect [non-spec.1.fix-design-G1.1] already rejected it for); a `keepCredentials` flag on `Release` (a dual mode); returning `bindAttempt` on `BindResult` for a fenced teardown (new field on three constructors to fix a same-session-two-slots-one-pod case that every unconditional Release already has).
EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/release.go:60-66 (Release → releaseCredentials); slotbinder.go:762-788 (ReleaseSlot → unconditional Shutdown, releaseCredentials, SlotClaimer.ReleaseSlot); resume.go:151 (bindAttempt local), :176-200 (no attempt field); credassign.go:400-408 (ReleaseSession releases all leases of the session); sessionserver/finalize.go:505-522 (terminal finalize releases them anyway); bind_rollback.go:20-33.
FACT: Release and ReleaseSlot send the UNCONDITIONAL teardown form (adapterclient sets unconditional_teardown), which the adapter reclaims regardless of bind-attempt token (pkg/adapter/session.go:383-401, rule 12). Only the fenced form (failResume, compensateAndNote) needs the token. All three BindResult constructors set `Adapter` (bindlaunch.go:96, slotbinder.go:386, resume.go:176).
WATCHOUT: On the rebuild branch the lost attempt's own leases (minted in Prepare) stay recorded under the session until the terminal `revokeFinalizeLease`; there is no per-attempt lease ID on `BindResult`. Do not "fix" this by releasing session-wide.
CORRECTS [non-spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]: its DECISION that item 5.3 "cites `failResume`, which already calls [releaseResumeSlot] after the pod-side reclaim" is wrong: failResume cannot run after a successful Resume (no token, connection-closing compensation) and does not fit the rebuild branch. Its OPEN (rebuild branch has no named release; Release/rollbackBinding revoke session-wide leases) is closed by ReleaseUncommitted.
USEFUL [non-spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]: its rejection of `Binder.Release`/`rollbackBinding` on credential grounds is what ruled out the finding's own suggested fix.
DECISION: The rebuild-branch publish in CODE-4 item 5.4 is `publishBinding`, item 4 adds `acquireCoordinationLease` (the checkpoint-branch acquire and its `rollbackBinding`) to what `resumeOnPod` no longer calls, and the item 3.3 self-renew sentence is deleted — BECAUSE item 3.3's acquire holds the lease to the commit (the Sweeper self-renews a held lease, CODE-4 item 14; `adoptedState` excludes `resuming`), and bind_registration.go:44-55 already forbids a second acquire after a running commit — ALTERNATIVES: keep `registerBinding` and roll back on error (the rolled-back row names a released pod).
DECISION: CODE-4 item 2 cites the Design lease gate; item 5.3's three releaser/orphan-GC sentences are deleted rather than replaced by a pointer; the shutdown edge case cuts the GC predicate to a citation of SPEC-1b **Exits from `resuming`.**.
WATCHOUT: The backoff test's discriminating assertion is "a `DriveResume` before the next-attempt time starts no attempt"; the jitter bound alone passes an implementation that records but never reads the time. `Options.Clock` (sessionserver.go:638) is the injection seam.

### [non-spec.2.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: the driver keeps 0091's release-before-rebind by calling `releaseThenResumeOnPod(ctx, row, onClaimed)` in CODE-4 item 3.4 (it gains the parameter and passes it to `resumeOnPod`); item 8 deletes "the `releaseThenResumeOnPod` call" — BECAUSE `releaseThenResumeOnPod` is the only caller of `releaseEarlierBinding`, and dropping it leaks a leftover binding that `completeResume` then publishes over — ALTERNATIVES: call `releaseEarlierBinding` inside `completeResume` (after the claim: the new claim already exists, and the slot lock order vs the funnel is the 0091 contract, rejected); inline the release in `attemptResume` (duplicates an existing helper).

### [non-spec.2.fix-design-G3.1]
DECISION: A PostAgentOutput REJECT on a held message records the §16.7 audit row, transcribes and publishes nothing for that entry, removes it, and the loop continues; an audit-row write failure is a loop error (entry stays) — BECAUSE the REJECT is the gate's outcome, not a delivery failure (leaving the entry would block every later path-2 Send behind it), and the HTTP path fails closed with 500 on an audit failure — ALTERNATIVES: a new rejection event type on the event stream (rejected: no such event exists in the tree, new protocol surface for a case the audit row already records); treating REJECT as a Send error (rejected: wedges the queue head).
FACT: The PostAgentOutput chain, its audit row, and the HTTP error envelope are one call chain through the response writer: recordDeliveredResponse -> runPostAgentOutput(ctx, w, ...) -> recordRouteRejection(ctx, w, ...) -> writeError(w, ...) which calls w.Header() and panics on a nil writer. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/messages_delivery.go:309-330, route.go:117-126, route.go:169-186, error_envelope.go:36-39
WATCHOUT: Moving the envelope write out of runPostAgentOutput breaks TestRunPostAgentOutputRejectReturns403, which asserts the 403 through runPostAgentOutput's recorder. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/postagentoutput_internal_test.go:33-37

On success the minted IDs are dropped today; `BindResult` has no lease field. EVIDENCE: podsession/bindrequest.go:130; slotbinder.go:553.

### [non-spec.3.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: `completeResume` (CODE-4 item 5) runs wholly on the server-lifetime context, and the attempt deadline (item 3.2, reset in item 3.4) bounds the attempt only up to the `completeResume` call — BECAUSE the deadline exists to bound how long a restore holds a pod, and a timer left armed past the commit cancels `deliverBuffered`'s `Send` mid-turn (PodExecutor.Send returns the ctx error and leaves the consumed turn to the reader, pkg/gateway/session/executor/pod.go:95-101), so the entry stays at the head and is re-sent with no crash or handoff, and it also cancels the fence, events, and `recoverDelegationTree` tail — ALTERNATIVES: timer.Stop() after the commit (an AfterFunc that already fired leaves the ctx cancelled); a ReleaseUncommitted exit on the step-1 lock wait (new exit clause, discards a restored pod); context.WithoutCancel per entry in deliverBuffered (closes only the double delivery); WithoutCancel(attemptCtx) for the tail (drops shutdown cancellation).
WATCHOUT: A post-deadline commit is safe only because step 2 is guarded on `resuming` and `G` and item 3.4 says correctness does not depend on the attempt ending before `sweepResuming`. Do not remove either when touching the deadline text. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-4 items 3.4 and 5.2.
OPEN: `resumeHeldAndDrain` (CODE-4 item 8) and the path-2 callers (CODE-6 item 7) run `deliverBuffered` on a request context, so a client disconnect mid-turn re-sends a held entry outside the RECORDS-1 7(i) duplicate allowance. Candidate remedy: send, record, and remove each entry on context.WithoutCancel(ctx) in CODE-4 item 6. Not landed here; it needs its own review.

### [non-spec.3.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: The off-holder held-pod `POST /resume` refusal (CODE-4 item 8) carries `currentState` and `coordinatingReplica` in its error details, the latter taken from the same `leaseStore.Get` the Design lease gate made and omitted when that read failed or found no lease — BECAUSE the §29.3 lead requires the typed refusal to carry the coordinating replica's identity (spec/29_communication-scenarios.md, "the typed refusal carries the identity of the session's coordinating replica") — ALTERNATIVES: narrowing the claim to "the §29.3 code and status" with a RECORDS-1 7(b) note (rejected: one clause closes it, the holder is already in hand); dropping the §29.3 attribution (rejected: the row is still governed by §29.3).
WATCHOUT: The Design lease gate reads the Redis `leasestore.LeaseStore` (`Server.leaseStore`), whose `Lease` names the replica as `Holder`. `coordlease.Lease.CoordinatorReplica` is the Postgres barrier mirror, which the session server does not read. Do not name `CoordinatorReplica` in CODE-4 item 8. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/leasestore/leasestore.go:70-76, :182; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/sessionserver.go:162.
FACT: `upload_to_session.go`'s TARGET_NOT_READY refusal sends only `currentState`, so it is not a precedent for the §29.3 forwarding-row refusal. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/upload_to_session.go:116-120.
OPEN: The spec names no details key for the coordinating-replica identity, and the other §29.3 forwarding rows that fail closed today carry none. This proposal names `coordinatingReplica` only for its own refusal; a spec-level key belongs to the §29.3/§15.1 owner.

### [non-spec.3.fix-G3.1]
DECISION: SPEC-5o's TTL default and re-arm get a code carrier in CODE-6 (item 10 flag, item 11 chart value, new item 14 with `(*Reserver).RearmTree` behind a `BudgetKeyRearmer` consumer interface, and a startup check that the TTL exceeds the suspended lifetime). RECORDS-1 7(d) and the summary Defects bullet now record only the §8.3 sentinel gap that predates this proposal — BECAUSE CODE-1 and CODE-6 let a root outlive the 24 h keys, and the reserve script reads a missing key as 0, so filing the gap left a quota reset reachable under default settings — ALTERNATIVES: building the §8.3 sentinel check here (needs a tree-creation marker because keys are lazy; it predates this proposal); sliding TTL on every reserve (changes the Lua contract, and an idle suspended tree still expires); re-arm from the §11.2 checkpoint loop (diverges from SPEC-5o); gating the re-arm on a root predicate (ParentSessionID is overloaded by derive; keying on row.ID matches nothing for a non-root).
FACT: The only treebudget constructor call is `treebudget.New(..., 0)` in cmd/lenny-gateway/session_messaging.go:143. The reserve script arms a TTL only when `TTL == -1` (treebudget.go:179-181). The `DefaultTTL` doc comment ("refreshed on every reserve", treebudget.go:38) is false, and item 14 rewrites it. PurgeRoot (treebudget.go:474) is the SCAN pattern RearmTree reuses. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegationtree/treebudget/treebudget.go
FACT: Delegation chart values render as env vars in templates/gateway-deployment.yaml (for example LENNY_DELEGATION_MAX_LEVEL_RECOVERY_SECONDS at :929), so `delegation.budgetKeyTTLSeconds` renders as LENNY_DELEGATION_BUDGET_KEY_TTL_SECONDS. EVIDENCE: charts/lenny/templates/gateway-deployment.yaml:929
WATCHOUT: Item 10's flag default and env var for `--delegation-budget-key-ttl-seconds` are meant to be covered by G4's flags_test.go clause ("each flag item 10 names"). When G3 ran, that clause was not yet in non-spec-changes.md. If G4 did not land it, the new flag has no default test. EVIDENCE: grep flags_test non-spec-changes.md
WATCHOUT: Never call `RearmTree` inside a store `Update` mutator, and never make a re-arm error fail the transition. The re-arm is post-commit and best-effort, and the next suspended entry or exit re-arms again. Until the §8.3 sentinel is built (RECORDS-1 7(d)), a missed re-arm next to a long non-suspended stretch can still expire keys silently.
MISTAKE: Earlier rounds recorded the missing TTL carrier as a pre-existing defect in RECORDS-1 7(d) and the summary. The proposal's own change made it reachable, so that record could not close it, and it cost a security finding in round 3.

### [non-spec.3.fix-G5.1]
CORRECTS [review-log WATCHOUT on SPEC-3b "state_change event"]: SPEC-3b's resume row is in the REST endpoint table, so the stream it means is the REST client event stream, whose frame is `status_change` (spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:140; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/lifecycle.go:221 publishEvent(..., "status_change", ...)). `state_change` is the adapter-facing SessionEventKind. ### [non-spec.3.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: `completeResume` runs on the server-lifetime context, so the attempt deadline ends at the call (CODE-4 item 5 lead sentence; item 3.4 "holds its pod" -> "holds its pod before `completeResume`") — BECAUSE the deadline exists to bound a restore that holds a pod, and every post-commit step (publish, fence, events, recoverDelegationTree, deliverBuffered) must not be cut by it; the commit is guarded on G, so a commit after the deadline is safe (item 3.4 already says correctness does not depend on the attempt ending first) — ALTERNATIVES: stop the timer after the commit and keep the attempt context (rejected: AfterFunc can fire between the commit and Stop, and Stop=false leaves a cancelled context; needs a fresh context anyway); keep step 5.1 on the attempt context and add a lock-wait release exit (rejected: adds an exit that discards a restored pod for a brief lock wait; the server context leaves only the shutdown exit, which **Driver goroutine at shutdown** already states).
FACT: PodExecutor.Send cannot tell "cancelled before the envelope was written" from "cancelled mid-turn": both return the context error, and a mid-turn cancel leaves the turn to the reader, so the runtime consumed it — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/executor/podstream.go runTurn/awaitReply; pkg/gateway/session/executor/pod.go Send doc comment. Any held-message Send on a cancellable context therefore risks double delivery on retry.
FACT: A turn with no caller deadline is still bounded by stream liveness: the adapter ends the stream with DEADLINE_EXCEEDED on a missed runtime heartbeat ack, and the end of the delivering request does not end the stream — EVIDENCE: spec/28_communication-channels.md CH-ATTACH **Timing.** and **Degradation.**
OPEN: Separate finding, not fixed in G1: deliverBuffered's other callers run it on a request context — `resumeHeldAndDrain` from `handleResume` (CODE-4 item 8) and the path-2 message paths (CODE-6 item 7, `SessionDelivery.DeliverHeld`). A client disconnect during a held-message turn cancels Send mid-turn, the entry stays at the head, and the next delivery point re-sends it: a duplicate with no crash or handoff, outside RECORDS-1 7(i). Candidate remedy in its one home, CODE-4 item 6: each entry's Send, record, and removal run on `context.WithoutCancel(ctx)`, and the loop checks ctx only before taking the next entry. That remedy would also make G1's completeResume context change cover shutdown-mid-turn.
OPEN: CODE-4 item 5 step 3 specifies only a failed guard; a store error on the step-2 commit (outcome unknown) has no stated disposition. Not raised by G1; a later round may file it.

### [non-spec.3.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: The off-holder held-pod `POST /resume` refusal (CODE-4 item 8) carries the lease holder's replica ID in its error details under `coordinatingReplica`, next to `currentState`, and omits it when the lease read failed or found no lease; the `upload_to_session.go` precedent sentence is deleted — BECAUSE the §29.3 lead requires the typed refusal to carry the coordinating replica's identity, and the lease read that refuses the request already returns `Lease.CoordinatorReplica` — ALTERNATIVES: narrowing the claim to "the §29.3 code and status" and recording the omission in RECORDS-1 7(b) (rejected: it is the failed claim made narrower, and it files a defect the proposal can close with one clause).
FACT: No gateway code emits the coordinating replica's identity on any TARGET_NOT_READY refusal; the §29.3 lead requires it for every forwarding row with an unreachable coordinator, and the spec names no details key for it. EVIDENCE: spec/29_communication-scenarios.md:550-553; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/upload_to_session.go:118-120; pkg/gateway/coordination/coordlease/coordlease.go:50-51.
OPEN: The other §29.3 forwarding rows that fail closed in the tree also omit the identity; the spec names no details key. Pre-existing, outside 0092; a later round or proposal should file it and fix the key in §15.1's error catalog.
WATCHOUT: The Design lease gate is stated as admit/refuse (non-spec-changes.md:10). Item 8 needs the holder value from the same read; do not add a second lease read for the refusal. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:100.

### [non-spec.3.fix-design-G3.1]
DECISION: The SPEC-5o budget-key TTL and re-arm get a carrier in CODE-6 (new item 14 plus the chart value in item 11): a `--delegation-budget-key-ttl-seconds` flag (default `treebudget.DefaultTTL`, raised to 691200 s) passed to `treebudget.New`, a startup refusal when it does not exceed `--max-suspended-session-seconds`, and a new `(*treebudget.Reserver).RearmTree` called after each committed write into or out of `suspended`. RECORDS-1 7(d) and the summary Defects bullet are replaced in place by the remaining pre-existing gap, the unbuilt §8.3 `BUDGET_KEYS_EXPIRED` sentinel — BECAUSE CODE-1 (active-time age) and CODE-6 (idle suspension, 604800 s suspended lifetime) make a root outlive the 24 h key TTL under defaults, which today the 7200 s wall-clock age cap prevents; the regression is this proposal's own, so recording it (round 2's answer) cannot close it — ALTERNATIVES: building the sentinel and the §8.3 cascade-cancel cleanup here (rejected: lazily created keys cannot tell a first reserve from an expired tree without a new tree-creation marker, plus a cascade path; a whole §8.3 feature, pre-existing, and unreachable under defaults once the TTL outlives each segment); a sliding TTL in the reserve script (rejected in an earlier round: changes the Lua contract and still expires an idle suspended tree); re-arming from the §11.2 checkpoint loop for every listed active tree (would keep keys alive for exactly the tree's life, but diverges from SPEC-5o, a spec-loop change); gating S13 on an external carrier (blocks the proposal on unfiled work).
FACT: Under defaults with the carrier, time between re-arms is bounded by one suspended period (≤ 604800 s) or one non-suspended period (active age ≤ 7200 s, since CODE-1 accrues only in starting/running/input_required, plus recovering windows of 900 s each), so the 691200 s TTL fires only for the SPEC-5 edge-case cycling root, a missed re-arm next to a long segment, or an operator `maxSessionAge` ≥ the TTL. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-1 item 2; spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:443.
WATCHOUT: `treebudget.DefaultTTL`'s doc comment says the TTL is "refreshed on every reserve"; the reserve script sets it only when `TTL == -1`. Do not rely on the comment. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegationtree/treebudget/treebudget.go:39-43, :179-181.
WATCHOUT: `RearmTree` keys on the session's own id for every row, root or not: a non-root has no `{id}:dlg:*` keys, so no root predicate is needed (`ParentSessionID` is overloaded by derive, sessionstore.go:143-151). It follows `PurgeRoot`'s SCAN of `{root}:dlg:*`. EVIDENCE: treebudget.go:474-500.
FACT: BUDGET_KEYS_EXPIRED appears in no Go code outside the alert description, and BUILD-GAPS.md has no finding for it; only TEST-GAPS.md (around :2615 and T-8.8.9) names it. EVIDENCE: pkg/alerting/rules/rules.go:571.
CORRECTS [round-2 fix "Correct the TTL statement at its three sites"]: correcting the statement left the regression in place; the finding needed a code carrier, not a better record.

DECISION: test additions cite their CODE item (3.7, 5.2, 9, 10) rather than restate the defaults or the counter rule — BECAUSE the test list asserts what the home gives — ALTERNATIVES: writing 900/604800 and the env var names into the test bullet (rejected: second statement of CODE-6 items 9-10; the CODE-4 backoff-flag clause is precedent but it restates, so it is not copied).
OPEN: whether a superseded attempt (item 3.6) or a lost commit (item 5.3) increments lenny_session_resume_attempts_total. CODE-4 states neither, and §16.1 says "every session resume attempt". The test asserts only the item 3.7 failure increment; a later round or the owner should decide and state it once in item 3.6/5.3 if it matters.

### [non-spec.3.fix-design-G5.1]
DECISION: SPEC-3b's `POST /v1/sessions/{id}/resume` row names the restore outcome a `status_change` event — BECAUSE the row sits in the REST endpoint table and the REST client event stream frame for a state transition is `status_change(state)` (spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:140; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/lifecycle.go publishEvent(..., "status_change", ...)); `state_change` is the SessionEventKind dispatched to external-protocol adapters (spec/15_external-api-surface.md) — ALTERNATIVES: keeping `state_change` and qualifying it as the adapter SessionEvent kind, rejected because the reader of a REST row is a REST client.
CORRECTS [spec.1 review-log WATCHOUT on SessionEvent kind vs status_change]: SPEC-3b is a REST endpoint row, so the stream it means is the REST client event stream and the correct frame name is `status_change`; the question that WATCHOUT left open is decided.

### [non-spec.3.follow-up-fix.1]
FACT: A `suspended` row also leaves `suspended` through the failure edge: `ReportSessionFailure` routes `running`, `input_required`, and `suspended` to `applyFailureFromActive`, which commits `resume_pending` (retryable, budget left) or `awaiting_client_action` (budget exhausted), or `failed` (terminal). Only the first two need a budget-key re-arm; a committed edge is `disp.To != from`. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/failure.go:154, :199-240
DECISION: Added `applyFailureFromActive` from `suspended` to CODE-6 item 14's exit-site list, `failure.go` to the CODE-6 Targets, and a failure-report case to the session-server rearmer test — BECAUSE SPEC-5o re-arms on every exit from `suspended` and the list was the only enumeration an implementor reads — ALTERNATIVES: rearming inside `transitionToResumePending`/`transitionToAwaitingClientAction` (rejected: shared with `running`/`input_required` sources, where a re-arm is not required).
WATCHOUT: Any new edge out of `suspended` must be added to CODE-6 item 14's exit-site list; the rearmer test enumerates those sites only. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 item 14, fourth bullet

### [non-spec.3.review-mechanism.1]
FACT: The attempt deadline (CODE-4 item 3.2, reset by the hook in item 3.4) cancels the attempt context, and nothing in CODE-4 item 5 stops it at the commit or moves completeResume onto another context. A held-message Send cut by a cancelled context leaves the turn to the reader and the entry at the head, so the next delivery point sends it again. FACT: Binder.Resume mints no credential lease (resume.go:260-262), so ReleaseUncommitted's MintedLeaseIDs being filled only by Bind/materializeSlot is correct for the checkpoint branch. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/resume.go:128-206.

FACT: The Sweeper's self-held renew (priorHolder == s.replicaID) keeps the lease of a bound-less session; releaseExecutor does not release the coordination lease. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:375; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/usage.go:34; coordination_lease.go:70 callers.
UNVERIFIED: completeResume's handling of a fenceResumedPod relinquish after the commit is unstated (before, the relinquish aborted the resume). Not filed; the fence onto a fresh pod relinquishes only on an exhausted transient budget. A later reviewer of CODE-4 item 5.4 may want to check it.

### [non-spec.3.review-reliability.1]
FACT: `executor.Send` returns the context error after the envelope is written and leaves the turn running on the runtime (the reader completes it), so any held entry whose `Send` ends on a cancelled context has been consumed but stays at the queue head and is re-sent at the next delivery point. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/executor/podstream.go:196-207, :233-251
WATCHOUT: `completeResume` runs on the attempt context, whose `time.AfterFunc` deadline (CODE-4 items 3.2 and 3.4, reset to `MaxResumingSeconds` at the hook) is never disarmed at the commit, so post-commit `deliverBuffered` turns are cut off by a pre-commit restore budget. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:66, :68, :74-80
WATCHOUT: The round-2 fix "audit-row failure leaves the held entry at the head" re-sends a message the runtime already answered; the REJECT verdict needs no redelivery to be audited. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:89; route.go:117-126; messages_delivery.go:323-330
FACT: The terminal DLQ drain runs on whichever replica writes the terminal (watchdog `recordCompleted` -> `OnSessionTerminal` -> `emitTerminalLifecycle` -> `drainMessagingOnTerminal`), so the `sweepSuspended` lifetime expiry reaches it. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go:1274-1277; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/usage.go:302-304, lifecycle.go:290
UNVERIFIED: a lost-commit `ReleaseUncommitted` on a slot pool with an already-cancelled attempt context (commit Update failing on the deadline) leaves the slot counted (ReleaseSlot uses the caller ctx; ReleaseSlotReservation detaches). Not filed; whoever owns the slot accounting should check. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/slotbinder.go:728-731, :762-781

### [non-spec.3.review-security.1]
FACT: Delegation budget counters are reset-on-expiry, not fail-closed: the reserve script reads each counter with `GET ... or '0'` and runs no EXISTS sentinel check, and it sets the TTL only when a key has none (`TTL == -1`), so the TTL is never refreshed after creation. The only constructor call passes ttl 0, which selects the 24 h DefaultTTL — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegationtree/treebudget/treebudget.go:43, :157, :179-181; cmd/lenny-gateway/session_messaging.go:143.
FACT: Today every non-terminal session, `suspended` included, expires at the wall-clock age cap (default 7200 s), so with default settings no tree root outlives the 24 h key TTL. CODE-1 (active-time age) plus CODE-6 (idle suspension, 604800 s suspended lifetime) is what makes a root outlive it — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go:112, :1084.
DECISION: Filed the budget-key reset as a security finding even though RECORDS-1 7(d) files the missing TTL carrier — BECAUSE filing the defect does not stop this proposal from making a silent quota reset reachable under default settings — ALTERNATIVES: treat it as an accepted residual (rejected, because the lens bar names a silent reset of a quota bound as a finding).
WATCHOUT: The lost-commit `ReleaseUncommitted` sends the unconditional-teardown Shutdown, while §4.7.1 rule 12 reserves the bind-attempt form for compensating reclaims. I did not file this. A successor attempt of the same session cannot hold an entry on the lost attempt's pod while that attempt's entry still stands (rule 5 refuses it), so I found no concrete failure — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:740; pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/slotbinder.go:762-783.
UNVERIFIED: In `ReleaseSuspendedPod`, the step-4 `releaseExecutor` goes through `Binder.Release`, whose `releaseCredentials` is session-wide. In principle it could revoke leases that a resume attempt started between step 3 and step 4 has minted. The window is milliseconds against at least one watchdog tick plus Prepare, so I did not file it. A reliability or concurrency reviewer should check it.

### [non-spec.4.follow-up-fix.1]
DECISION: CODE-6 item 1 stages the consumers of the rerouted `lenny/interrupt_session` result (Go SDK `InterruptSession` decodes `Session`, `cmdSessionInterrupt` prints `ID`, both fakes return the REST body); Targets and Files touched list `sdks/client/go/lenny/mcp.go`, `mcp_test.go`, and `pkg/embedded/localcli/session.go`, `session_verbs_test.go` — BECAUSE `SessionResponse` keys the identifier `id` (session_response.go:18) and `MCPSessionState` reads `sessionId` (mcp.go:365) — ALTERNATIVES: keep the MCP handler (rejected: a second interrupt writer).

### [non-spec.4.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: `deliverBuffered` (CODE-4 item 6) runs each entry's `Send`, `recordDeliveredResponse`, and removal on the server-lifetime context and reads the caller's `ctx` only between entries — BECAUSE `PodExecutor.Send` returns the context error mid-turn after the runtime consumed the message, so a request-context caller (handleResume, path-2 REST, MCP DeliverHeld, ResumeSuspended) left a consumed entry at the head and the next delivery point re-sent it with a fresh envelope ID — ALTERNATIVES: `context.WithoutCancel` (escapes shutdown); moving each caller to the server-lifetime context (one rule at several sites); deriving the envelope ID from MessageID (no adapter dedup set exists). This closes the OPEN client-disconnect re-send entries in the round-3 log. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/executor/pod.go:95-101, :110-116.
WATCHOUT: Do not add per-caller detached contexts for held delivery; the rule's one home is CODE-4 item 6, and `completeResume`'s server-lifetime context (item 5) is a separate need (lock wait and commit write).
DECISION: The REST podless-held failure cases extend `TestPodlessMessageEnqueuesBeforeResumePending`. The design's text said "the row left `suspended`" for both cases; a `terminal` re-read means the row is already terminal, so only the error case asserts `suspended`.
DECISION: Tier-8 `TestCoordinatorKillHeldMessageDeliveredByPeer` asserts at-least-once delivery and an empty DLQ (RECORDS-1 7(i) allows a crash duplicate). WATCHOUT: in the held-pod case the message must be sent after the kill through the surviving replica (lease gate refuses it, so it is held and answers `queued`); a message sent to the live holder resumes synchronously and never exercises adoption.

### [non-spec.4.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: The MCP `lenny/interrupt_session` handler is deleted and the tool re-registers through `postByID(..., "/interrupt")` in client_tools.go (CODE-6 item 1) — BECAUSE the MCP handler was a second writer into `suspended` that stamped neither `SuspendedAt` nor `SuspendedReason`, skipped the adapter interrupt, emitted no status change, and would miss the item 14 re-arm; routing through `handleInterrupt` makes item 14's site list, items 2.1/2.2/2.3, and the item 4.3 `SuspendedAt` guard true as written — ALTERNATIVES: stamping the fields in the MCP mutator and listing it in item 14 (keeps a second copy of handleInterrupt); store-level stamping (new mechanism, still no adapter signal or re-arm).
FACT: Before this fix the MCP interrupt wrote only `row.State = StateSuspended`; a zero `SuspendedAt` would make `sweepSuspended` expire the row on the next tick. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcptools/mcptools_register.go:524-557; contrast pkg/gateway/sessionserver/interrupt.go:83-89. Playground calls it: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/playground/ui/app.js:933.
WATCHOUT: Do not keep both registrations; `RegisterTool` overwrites by name, so registration order would pick the handler. Client tools register only when `SessionService` is wired; production always wires it (cmd/lenny-gateway/mcpsurface.go). Do not add zero-`SuspendedAt` guards to items 2.1/2.3/4.3.
DECISION: Gateway wiring of the turn admitter and the budget rearmer goes through two named cmd/lenny-gateway helpers, `wireTurnAdmitter(w.exec, activityStamper)` (type-asserts `*executor.PodExecutor` as main.go does for `SetStreamFailureHandler`) and `budgetKeyRearmer(*treebudget.Reserver)` (untyped nil for nil), pinned by `TestTurnAdmitterAndBudgetRearmerWiring` — BECAUSE every other test builds its own fixture, so an unwired seam passes them all — ALTERNATIVES: full buildStores/buildSessionDeps test (needs k8s and Postgres); tier 4/7a only (none reaches the race or the re-arm).
WATCHOUT: A `*treebudget.Reserver(nil)` assigned to the `BudgetKeyRearmer` interface is a typed nil that passes `!= nil` and panics in `RearmTree`; that is the wiring test's discriminating assertion. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/session_messaging.go:140-146,231 (`treeBudgetConcrete` set only with Redis).

### [non-spec.4.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: `deliverBuffered` runs each entry's `Send`, `recordDeliveredResponse`, and removal on the server-lifetime context (CODE-4 item 2), and reads its caller's `ctx` only before taking the next entry; the rule lives once in CODE-4 item 6 and covers every caller (completeResume, resumeHeldAndDrain from handleResume and ResumeSuspended, path-2 REST, MCP DeliverHeld, ResumeBacklog) — BECAUSE PodExecutor.Send returns the ctx error mid-turn while the runtime has consumed the message (pod.go Send doc comment; podstream.go awaitReply), and each envelope gets a fresh `newMessageID()`, so a request/tool-call cancel left a consumed entry at the head and the next delivery point re-sent it — ALTERNATIVES: `context.WithoutCancel(ctx)` (rejected: never cancelled at shutdown, so a handler blocks graceful shutdown until the stream ends, and it is a second context rule beside completeResume's); moving each caller onto the server-lifetime context (rejected: one rule stated at five sites); deriving the envelope ID from the held MessageID for adapter dedup (rejected: no `delivered_message_ids` set exists, RECORDS-1 7(i)).
DECISION: A shutdown that cancels the server-lifetime context mid-turn still leaves the consumed entry at the head; it is redelivered by the next coordinator, which is the crash/handoff duplicate RECORDS-1 7(i) already files. Not restated in staged text.
FACT: A turn on the server-lifetime context is still bounded: `awaitReply` returns on stream end (`c.done`), which the adapter heartbeat escalation and 0091's CH-ATTACH stream-failure rule produce for a hung runtime. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/executor/podstream.go awaitReply.
WATCHOUT: Test the context rule once, at `deliverBuffered` (TestDeliverBufferedCallerCancelMidTurnDeliversOnce); do not add per-caller variants through handleResume and DeliverHeld, because no caller chooses the context any more.
WATCHOUT: Tier-8 held-pod `suspended` kill: a message sent to the holder would resume synchronously and never be held across the kill. Send it through the surviving replica after the kill and before the lease lapses (non-holder answers `queued`, writes nothing), so it is in the DLQ when the peer adopts. Assert at-least-once (RECORDS-1 7(i)) plus an empty DLQ.
DECISION: This closes the review-log OPEN "A client disconnect during a held-message turn cancels Send mid-turn ..." (review-log ledger near :1797, :1827).

### [non-spec.4.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: `lenny/interrupt_session` becomes a `postByID(..., "/interrupt")` proxy in client_tools.go and its own handler in mcptools_register.go is deleted, so `handleInterrupt` is the one interrupt write into `suspended` — BECAUSE it deletes the second writer instead of teaching it the stamps, the re-arm, the adapter signal and the status event; item 14's site list and the review-log `SuspendedAt` release-guard decision then hold as written — ALTERNATIVES: stamp `SuspendedAt`/`SuspendedReason` in the MCP mutator and add it to item 14 (a second copy of handleInterrupt that still skips the §4.7 adapter interrupt and `emitStatusChange`, and drifts again).
FACT: the MCP `lenny/interrupt_session` handler never calls the adapter's interrupt and never emits a status change; only handleInterrupt does. After the deletion `transitionInterrupt` has one caller. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcptools/mcptools_register.go:524-557; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/interrupt.go:66-95; session_transitions.go:119.
FACT: only cmd/lenny-gateway/mcpsurface.go:224 calls mcptools.Register in production, and it wires SessionService (:234), so a postByID tool is always registered in the deployed gateway; the unit suite without a SessionService does not register it (client_tools_test.go TestClientToolsRegisteredWithService_spec_15_2_3).

### [non-spec.4.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: Filed the request-context held-message delivery as a finding (the OPEN at review-log G1, "resumeHeldAndDrain ... and the path-2 callers ... run deliverBuffered on a request context") — BECAUSE it is the same defect the round-3 fix closed for completeResume, the consumed entry stays at the head and is re-sent with no crash or handoff, and `PodExecutor.Send` mints a fresh envelope ID per send (`newMessageID()`), so even an implemented adapter `delivered_message_ids` set would not suppress the re-send — ALTERNATIVES: leaving it OPEN (rejected: it meets the bar and has a one-home remedy in CODE-4 item 6). EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/executor/pod.go:95-133; non-spec-changes.md:85, :99, :154, :157
FACT: `treebudget` reserve script arms a key's TTL only when `TTL == -1`; only `GrantTokenBudget`, the high-watermark SET, and `Restore` refresh it. The doc comment "refreshed on every reserve" is wrong today. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegationtree/treebudget/treebudget.go:38-43, :172-195, :454-458; checkpoint.go:69-78
FACT: A single failed budget-key re-arm (CODE-6 item 14) does not expire keys mid-suspension at the defaults: time outside `suspended` is bounded by the 2 h age cap plus the 900 s windows, and the TTL exceeds the suspended lifetime by 1 day. Two consecutive failed re-arms are needed. Judged below the bar. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go:112; spec-changes.md:622
WATCHOUT: `sweepMaxAge`'s mutator returns nil on a concurrent terminal and then notifies on `updated.State == expired`, so a peer's expiry is re-notified and `recordCompleted` runs twice. CODE-6 item 2.1 says `sweepSuspended` notifies "as sweepMaxAge does"; an implementor copying that pattern inherits the double. Not filed (pre-existing pattern, guard wording implies a sentinel). EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go:1087-1114; non-spec-changes.md:136
UNVERIFIED: `PurgeRoot` (and the new `RearmTree` modelled on it) SCAN through a `redis.UniversalClient`; on a Redis Cluster client the SCAN may not reach the root's slot node. Pre-existing; whoever owns the delegation Redis topology should check.
USEFUL [review-log G1 OPEN at :1827]: named the request-context delivery gap and its one-home remedy; saved the trace.

### [non-spec.5.review-mechanism.1]

FACT: `PodExecutor.Release` goes through `unbind`, which evicts the cached Attach stream with `closedByGateway` set, so the CODE-6 item 4 zero-hold release does not produce a stream-failure report that would move the `suspended` row to `resume_pending`. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/executor/pod.go:180-209, :257-273
FACT: Every DLQ enqueue in the tree scores `EnqueuedAt + DefaultDLQTTL`, and `EnqueuedAt` is stamped at enqueue time after the row read that classified the message (messages_inbox.go:45-48, mcptools_register.go:367-379, coordinator.go:126). So `CountAcceptedAfter(SuspendedAt)` counts every message classified against the `suspended` row, apart from cross-replica clock skew. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessioninbox/dlq.go:87-95
FACT: `lenny/interrupt_session` through `postByID` reaches `handleInterrupt` through `ServiceCall`, which serves the full `s.Handler()` mux. `mcpsurface.go:234` is the only production `mcptools.Deps`, and it wires `SessionService`, so moving the tool behind the SessionService check removes it only in unit fixtures. The playground (`playground/ui/app.js:933`) sends the tool and does not parse its result. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/service.go:59-103
WATCHOUT: `deliverBuffered` on a non-coordinating replica, reached through the REST path-2 call that is not lease-gated, fails in `streamFor` ("not bound") before the admitter runs and leaves the entry in place, so this is not a delivery defect. Do not refile it as one. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/executor/pod.go:102-106, :150-158
WATCHOUT: Between two held entries of one `deliverBuffered` loop, `TurnInFlight` is false (each entry is its own `Send`). That gap is safe because the next `Send`'s `StampSync` refuses a `suspended` row and the entry stays, and the next tick's `Idle` backlog resume delivers it. Do not refile it as a mid-delivery suspension.
UNVERIFIED: `sweepSuspended` item 2.1 copies the `sweepMaxAge` pattern ("as sweepMaxAge does"). With per-replica watchdogs, a replica whose guard misses on an already-`expired` row may still see `updated.State == expired` and notify a second time. This predates the proposal in `sweepMaxAge` (watchdog.go:1087-1113), and I did not report it.

### [non-spec.5.review-reliability.1]
FACT: The watchdog runs unconditionally on every replica (cmd/lenny-gateway/workers.go:66), and the existing expiry writers `sweepMaxAge` and `sweepIdle` return nil from the mutator on a concurrent transition, then notify on `updated.State == expired` (watchdog.go:1087-1113, :1020-1043). A second replica whose listing is stale therefore re-runs `OnSessionExpired` and `OnSessionTerminal` (usage.go:302, :352: billing event, final snapshot, executor release, expiry counter) for a row another replica already expired. CODE-6 item 2.1 copies this pattern "as sweepMaxAge does". Filed this round.
WATCHOUT: CODE-4 item 15 already fixes the same nil-on-miss idiom for `sweepResuming` with a sentinel; any new watchdog write that notifies after a guarded Update needs the same sentinel. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:108, :136
UNVERIFIED [weighed]: The earlier UNVERIFIED note on `sweepDLQExpiry` listing `resuming` (review-log.md:1603) is still below the bar. The sweep re-checks no state (watchdog.go:688-702; dlq.go:142-148), but a double outcome needs an entry that crossed its score within one tick before the commit, plus a stale `resuming` listing whose SweepExpired call lands after `deliverBuffered`'s Peek. Expiry alone after the commit is an acceptable outcome (expired with message_expired). Not filed.
FACT: `PodExecutor.Send` has no turn timeout. It waits until the reply arrives, ctx ends, or the stream ends (podstream.go:224-255). After the round-4 change, `deliverBuffered` holds the delivery lock on the server-lifetime context for the length of a held turn. The turn token already serializes the next Send behind that turn, so this adds no new stall.

### [non-spec.5.review-security.1]
DECISION: security lens returned no findings on non-spec round 5 — BECAUSE the r4->r5 changes keep every control intact: `lenny/interrupt_session` via `postByID` dispatches through `ServiceCall` with the caller's ctx and `callerTenantID` (the same path `lenny/resume_session` already uses), so tenant scoping is unchanged; the server-lifetime context for held-entry Send/record/remove does not drop an audit identity, because `runPostAgentOutput` passes tenantID explicitly and an empty CallerSub today; `wireTurnAdmitter` type-asserts an unwrapped `*executor.PodExecutor`. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcptools/client_tools.go:47-71; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/service.go:85-103; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/route.go:169-190; cmd/lenny-gateway/executor_credentials.go:153; cmd/lenny-gateway/main.go:191.
WATCHOUT: Several earlier FACT entries (review-log :689, :887, :1212) say budget-key expiry "fails closed" through the BUDGET_KEYS_EXPIRED sentinel. That is true of the spec only. The code's `reserveScript` reads a missing key as 0 and recreates it, so an expiry silently resets the counters. The proposal already files this as RECORDS-1 item 7(d), so it is not a new finding. Do not refile it, and do not cite the sentinel as an implemented control. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegationtree/treebudget/treebudget.go:154-195; non-spec-changes.md RECORDS-1 7(d).
CORRECTS [review-log :1212]: the "cannot silently reset a budget ceiling" claim holds for the spec, but not for the tree until RECORDS-1 7(d) is closed.

### [non-spec.6.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: The no-retry-spend rule for non-failure entries into `resume_pending` is pinned by one discriminating case on the `handleResume` bullet (row seeded at `RetryCount == maxRetries`) — BECAUSE CODE-4 item 9 routes every non-failure entry through `enterResumePending`, and today's resume path spends a retry (session_generation.go:75, :85) — ALTERNATIVES: a second case on TestPodlessMessageEnqueuesBeforeResumePending (one rule, two cases); a direct unit test of `enterResumePending` (passes when a caller bypasses the helper).
DECISION: The `lenny/interrupt_session` result contract is pinned at tier 3 by extending the existing parity clause into `tests/tier3_contract/rest_mcp_consistency` — BECAUSE that harness wires `SessionService: rest` (consistency_test.go:61); the sdks harness wires none, so the tool would be absent there.
WATCHOUT: Replaced: per-outcome enumerations in TestPodlessMessageEnqueuesBeforeResumePending and TestSendMessageToSuspendedEnqueuesDLQAndResumes. Each round appended the next missing result (terminal, error, then dropped/dlq_overflow) and the next round found one more. Both tests are now table-driven over every hold result and point at CODE-6 item 7's REST and MCP bullets; do not re-enumerate outcomes in the test bullets.

DECISION: The `lenny/interrupt_session` tier-3 parity and SDK decode go in `tests/tier3_contract/rest_mcp_consistency`, whose harness wires `SessionService: rest` — BECAUSE `newMCPGateway` in tests/tier3_contract/sdks/go_client_test.go:70-81 wires no SessionService, so `postByID` tools are absent there — EVIDENCE: consistency_test.go `newConsistencyServers` (SessionService: rest); interrupt.go:119-125 (no pod registry binding falls through to the row-only transition, so a seeded `running` row interrupts in the harness).

### [non-spec.6.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: Returned no findings under the reliability lens for non-spec round 6. BECAUSE the round-5 diff (the CODE-6 item 2.1 `errSuspendedSuperseded` miss handling, the expiry test that asserts the terminal-hook calls, and `TestSendMessageBufferedRunsPreMessageDelivery` widened to the `input_required` inbox branch) changes no recovery mechanism. I re-traced every held-message edge on and off the coordinating replica and found no new loss, double-apply, leak, or stall. The edges were: idle suspension with the backlog check ahead of the zero-hold release; the release racing a held-pod resume through the `holdsPod` guard and `errResumeHeldPodReleased`; the podless and held-pod resume; admission refusal and re-route after the suspend write; the commit followed by `deliverBuffered`; a coordinator crash after the `running` commit; a serving-replica crash between the DLQ enqueue and the state write; and an enqueue racing a terminal write. ALTERNATIVES: (1) The expiry sweep's stale `resuming` listing double outcome. Rejected because it was already weighed twice (review-log.md:1603, :2048). (2) Clock skew in the interrupt backlog predicate. Rejected because it was already weighed (review-log.md:975, :1372). (3) The `ReleaseSuspendedPod` step-3 guard carries no `coordination_generation`, so a replica that lost its lease mid-checkpoint can still clear `PodAssignment` after a peer re-adopted the pod. Rejected as below the bar: it needs the lease to be lost while the replica is still alive and a peer to adopt within the 60 s checkpoint. If it happens, the session recovers through the orphan-claim GC and the stream-failure edge.
FACT: `sweepDLQExpiry` lists rows by state and calls `SweepExpired` with no state re-check, and `SweepExpired` is an atomic Lua take. Any race between expiry and delivery therefore comes from the stale listing alone. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go:684-705; pkg/gateway/session/sessioninbox/dlq.go:156-163
FACT: The 0091 Sweeper keeps the lease of a session with no binding through the `priorHolder == s.replicaID` renew. A podless `suspended` or `resume_pending` session therefore keeps its coordinator, which is what the CODE-4 item 14 lease-gate dependency and the backlog-resume gate rely on. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:375, :427
USEFUL [review-log:2048]: it saved re-deriving the expiry-versus-delivery race.

### [non-spec.10.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: An unacknowledged completeResume commit is closed by making the step-2 Update idempotent (success when CoordinationGeneration == G and RecoveryGeneration is one above the item 3.1 read) and repeating it on any non-guard store error until it succeeds, the guard fails, or the server-lifetime context ends — BECAUSE after bumpRecoveryGeneration is deleted the commit is the only RecoveryGeneration writer, so the predicate identifies this commit exactly, and every outcome reduces to the existing commit or guard-miss branches — ALTERNATIVES: re-read with store.Get and continue when running at G naming the sandbox (adds a third branch; misreads a same-generation write after the landed commit and releases a still-resuming row's pod), publish before the commit (reopens the guard-miss published-binding case), route the error to the item 3.7 report (its resuming guard misses on a landed commit and the same replica's Sweeper never re-adopts, coordination.go:375,:427).
WATCHOUT: pgstore.Update can return an error after the COMMIT landed (pgtenant.go:98-100 wraps tx.Commit's error). Never treat a non-guard Update error in completeResume as a guard miss: ReleaseUncommitted would free a pod the committed row names. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/pgtenant/pgtenant.go:98
DECISION: CODE-4 item 4 now deletes bumpRecoveryGeneration and points at item 5.4 for publish and fence — BECAUSE its only caller is resume_rebind.go:129, which item 4 removes, and tier 0 `unused` (tests: true, .golangci.yml:12,:21) would fail at S12; item 3.3 is the one lease acquire and item 5.2 the one increment — ALTERNATIVES: calling it from completeResume (double increment), deleting it in CODE-2 at S8 (its caller still exists until S12).
CORRECTS [review-log FACT at :1895 "only retry_metric_internal_test.go keeps it alive"]: no test calls bumpRecoveryGeneration; retry_metric_internal_test.go:90 names it only in a comment and calls recordSessionRetry. After item 4 it has no caller at all.
OPEN: comments crediting bumpRecoveryGeneration with retry accounting or naming it as a path (watchdog.go:351, :829; usage.go:334; gatewaymetrics.go:502; retry_metric_internal_test.go:90) are named by no CODE-2 or CODE-4 item; once CODE-4 deletes the function they name a missing symbol. A later round should add them to an edit list.

### [non-spec.10.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: held-message store carries one encoding: the MCP buffering block in mcptools_register.go marshals a `sessionserver.MessagePayload{ID, Role:"user", Content}` instead of the bare MessageContent union, and deliverBuffered decodes only MessagePayload through a helper extracted from parseMessageBatch's projection — BECAUSE one writer change removes the second format at its source — ALTERNATIVES: content-sniffing two-branch decoder (keeps two formats forever); new envelope type in sessioninbox (duplicates MessagePayload); remove/dead-letter an undecodable entry (drops messages under at-least-once).

WATCHOUT: the MCP block's comment (F-MS5) insists the union is buffered in wire form; MessagePayload.Content keeps that form (it is the same sessionrecord.MessageContent), so the implementor rewrites the marshal and comment, not the content type — EVIDENCE: mcptools_register.go:358-366
OPEN: a decode failure in deliverBuffered still stops the loop with the entry at the head; with one writer format no producer emits one, but nothing removes a corrupt entry from a running session's DLQ.

### [non-spec.10.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: An unacknowledged completeResume commit is resolved by making the commit Update idempotent and repeating it, rather than by a re-read branch — BECAUSE the mutator can recognise its own landed write exactly (`CoordinationGeneration == G` and `RecoveryGeneration` one above the item 3.1 read; after CODE-4 the commit is the only `recovery_generation` writer and any other commit needs its own entry increment), so every repeat ends in the two outcomes step 3 already has — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's re-read with predicate "running at G with PodAssignment == sandbox" (rejected: an interrupt suspension or other same-generation write after the landed commit falsifies it and the attempt then releases a pod the row names; it also adds a third branch to step 3).
FACT: After CODE-4 deletes `bumpRecoveryGeneration`, the completeResume commit is the only writer of `RecoveryGeneration` in pkg/ — EVIDENCE: grep `RecoveryGeneration++` finds only pkg/gateway/sessionserver/session_generation.go:74; pgstore.Update returns the tx.Commit error (pkg/gateway/storage/pgtenant/pgtenant.go:98-100).
FACT: A `running` row whose lease lapsed is re-adopted by a peer Sweeper (`adoptable := leaseUnheld && isRunningPod(row)`), so a shutdown during the commit repeat is recovered; the same replica never re-adopts while it renews — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:342, :375.
WATCHOUT: Comments in pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go:351 and :829, pkg/gateway/sessionserver/usage.go:334, pkg/gateway/metrics/gatewaymetrics/gatewaymetrics.go:502, and pkg/gateway/sessionserver/retry_metric_internal_test.go:90 credit `bumpRecoveryGeneration` with gateway-initiated retry accounting. They go false at CODE-2 item 1 (S8), not at the deletion; no CODE-2 item rewrites them. Separate finding for a later round.

### [non-spec.10.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: Close the two-encoding hazard by storing ONE encoding: the MCP buffering block in mcptools_register.go marshals `sessionserver.MessagePayload{ID: messageID, Role: "user", Content: in.Message}` (the §15.4 MessageEnvelope that messages_inbox.go's doc comment already says the buffer holds), and deliverBuffered decodes every entry as a MessagePayload through the projection deliverMessageBatch already applies (role default `user`, `Content.Text()`), extracted as one helper — BECAUSE one writer format and one decoder removes the mismatch instead of specifying a sniffer for it — ALTERNATIVES: content-sniffing decoder (object => REST, else MCP union) as the finding suggests: keeps two encodings forever and adds a second decode branch (hair); a new shared envelope type in sessioninbox: duplicates MessagePayload; a migration/dual-read for already-stored entries: no deployments exist.
FACT: mcptools already imports sessionserver (pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcptools/client_tools.go:14), so the MCP block can marshal sessionserver.MessagePayload without a new dependency. The REST projection to executor.Message is pkg/gateway/sessionserver/messages_delivery.go:73-88. No test asserts the bare-union MCP payload form (grep of mcptools tests for Payload/F-MS5 is empty). — EVIDENCE: mcptools_register.go:358-371; messages_inbox.go:30-48
USEFUL [non-spec.3.review-fresh.1]: the WATCHOUT on two payload encodings was accurate and located both writers.
OPEN: a payload that fails to decode (corruption, not encoding mismatch) still stops deliverBuffered with the entry at the head under item 6's error rule, and nothing in a running session removes it. With one encoding this is not reachable from either writer; whether a decode failure should remove-and-audit the entry is a question for a later round, not this fix.

### [non-spec.11.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: CODE-4 item 5.3 is the one home of the commit-miss classification; item 5.2 only retries on a store error. A miss counts as landed only when it follows a store error and the row the guard read is in neither a terminal nor a recovery state and names the attempt's sandbox; at `G` run step 4 (steps 5 and 6 only when `running`), above `G` publish and release nothing (CODE-5 item 2 readopt owns the pod); every other miss calls `ReleaseUncommitted` — BECAUSE the round-10 success conjunction (running ∧ sandbox ∧ G ∧ RG+1) released a pod a live row named after a row-only interrupt `suspended` write or a takeover `RecordHandoff` — ALTERNATIVES: reviewer's `G` ∧ RG+1 witness (fails under takeover, needs the item 3.1 baseline the binderless path lacks); RG-above-baseline witness on the binder path (attempt B's commit raises RG too, so A leaks); guarding the interrupt fallback (closes path (a) only).
WATCHOUT: The design as supplied classified EVERY miss by the sandbox witness. On a concurrent pool `PodAssignment` is the shared pod name (pod_claim.go ClaimResult doc: SandboxName persisted on row.PodAssignment), so attempt A's clean miss against attempt B's commit on another slot of the same pod would read "landed above G" and leak A's slot and leases; and a recovery-state row keeps the failed pod's name (failure.go stamps rep.PodAssignment; pod_launch.go:183-192), so a watchdog-exited row at `G` could read "landed at G" and publish a binding on `resume_pending`. Hence the two added conditions (after a store error; not a recovery state). Do not drop either. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/pod_claim.go:85-90; failure.go:337-338
FACT: At `G`, the only non-recovery, non-terminal successor of the `resuming` row is A's own commit: interrupt admits no `resuming` row, failure reporters need a published binding (none before step 4), and `sweepResuming` exits to `resume_pending` without a bump. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/interrupt.go:55-58 (EndpointInterrupt precondition); session_transitions.go:119
OPEN: Residual after a lost ack on a concurrent pool: landed, adopted at G+1, then failed to `resume_pending` naming the sandbox, item 5.3 calls `ReleaseUncommitted` on a pod the next attempt's `releaseEarlierBinding` also releases (possible double `ReleaseSlot`); and a lost-ack miss against attempt B's commit on the same pod reads as landed-above-G and leaks A's slot. Both need ack loss plus a watchdog exit or takeover inside one commit retry loop. A reliability lens should decide whether a per-attempt witness (none exists on the row today) is warranted.
OPEN: A landed commit whose row is already `suspended` skips the item 5.5 tail (no `session.resumed` audit, no status change), per the design; an audit-completeness lens should confirm that is acceptable.
DECISION: Binderless clause (CODE-4 item 2) substitutes `RecoveryGeneration` above the row `DriveResume` received for the sandbox and `G`, and takes the `G` branch — BECAUSE after CODE-4 item 4 deletes `bumpRecoveryGeneration` only a `completeResume` commit raises it, and binderless rows have no `PodAssignment`, so CODE-5 never readopts them.

### [non-spec.11.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: a `HeldLen` error in `sweepSuspended` item 2.2 skips the row for the tick (no ResumeBacklog, no release) and does not propagate — BECAUSE `Tick` returns on the first per-row error and would stall every later sweep and tenant during a Redis reset — ALTERNATIVES: propagate (stalls lifetime expiry, sweepMaxAge, sweepIdle, state timeouts); treat as zero (releases an Idle row's pod while its backlog is unreadable).
FACT: `sweepDLQExpiry` is the only Tick sweep that drops a per-session error; every other sweep and the per-state loop return the first store error out of `Tick` — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go `Tick` and `sweepDLQExpiry` (`if serr != nil { continue }`).
WATCHOUT: the discriminating test row must be an `Idle` row holding a pod; a podless or interrupt row lets a treat-as-zero implementation pass — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md TestSweepSuspendedResumesBacklog.
OPEN: item 2.1's Postgres Update error and other per-row errors in `sweepSuspended` have no stated outcome; a sweep-wide skip rule was not adjudicated this round.

### [non-spec.11.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: A `completeResume` guard miss is classified by whether the row the guard read names the attempt's sandbox (CODE-4 item 5.3), on every miss rather than only on a repeat: names it at `G` and non-terminal → publish (step 4), tail and delivery only when `running`; names it above `G` and non-terminal → a peer's readopt owns the pod, do nothing; otherwise (terminal, or names no/another pod) → `ReleaseUncommitted` — BECAUSE the replacement sandbox is written into `PodAssignment` only by this attempt's commit, so naming it is a state-independent witness that the commit landed, and the release decision then follows CODE-6 item 4's invariant directly — ALTERNATIVES: the round-10 conjunctive repeat-success predicate (`running` ∧ sandbox ∧ `G` ∧ `RecoveryGeneration` = base+1), which released the pod on any later state change (interrupt row-only `suspended`, takeover to G+1); a `RecoveryGeneration`-above-base witness on the binder path (wrong: attempt A at G, watchdog exit, attempt B commits at G+1 → A would see RG above base and leak its pod).
WATCHOUT: `handleInterrupt`'s row-only fallback writes `suspended` with no state guard in its mutator and keeps `PodAssignment`, and it takes no delivery lock, so it can land between a lost-ack commit and the repeat. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/interrupt.go:63-66, :84-89; session_transitions.go `transitionInterrupt`.
FACT: The only `RecoveryGeneration` increment in the tree is `bumpRecoveryGeneration` (session_generation.go:74), which CODE-4 item 4 deletes; after 0092 only `completeResume`'s step 2 bumps it. This is what makes the binderless witness (RG above the row `DriveResume` received) sound. EVIDENCE: grep `RecoveryGeneration++` pkg cmd.
OPEN: A landed commit whose row is no longer `running` at the miss (interrupt or idle suspension before the repeat) runs no item 5.5 tail, so no `session.resumed` audit row or resumed event is written for a resume that did occur. Chosen because the tail announces a `running` session after a later status change already reached the client; an audit-completeness lens may want only the counter and audit row to run.

### [non-spec.11.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: A `HeldLen` error skips the row for that tick (no `ResumeBacklog`, no release) and is not returned from `sweepSuspended`, stated as one sentence appended to CODE-6 item 2.2 that points at `sweepDLQExpiry` — BECAUSE propagating aborts the serial per-tenant `Tick` (later sweeps and later tenants), and treating the error as zero lets sub-item 3 release an `Idle` row's pod with an unread backlog — ALTERNATIVES: propagate as `Tick` does for store errors (rejected: stalls every sweep during a Redis reset); treat as zero (rejected: releases a pod whose backlog is unknown); a sweep-wide "every per-row error skips the row" rule at item 2 (rejected here: also re-scopes item 2.1's store Update error, which no finding examined; see OPEN).
FACT: `Tick` returns on the first per-row store error in every sweep except `sweepDLQExpiry`, which drops a per-session `SweepExpired` error and continues. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go:621-626 (Tick), :791-793 (sweepResuming), :684-703 (sweepDLQExpiry).
WATCHOUT: The test case for this rule must use a held-pod `Idle` row (hold zero) as the failing row: only then does a treat-as-zero implementation release the pod, so "keeps its pod" is the discriminating assertion; "the second row is evaluated and `Tick` returns nil" discriminates against propagation. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 item 2.3.
OPEN: CODE-6 item 2.1 does not say what a non-sentinel `Update` error does in `sweepSuspended`; the tree's pattern (sweepResuming) propagates a Postgres error, which is consistent with every other store sweep, so it is probably not a finding, but nobody has adjudicated it.

Contradicts CODE-6 item 4 (non-spec:146) and summary decision 8. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:76-77, :272

### [non-spec.11.review-performance.1]
FACT: go-redis v9.19.0 routes a keyless command (SCAN) on a ClusterClient to a round-robin master (osscluster.go:2361-2366 cmdNodeWithShardPicker; osscluster_router.go:276-283 sends SCAN's special policy to executeDefault), and no ClusterClient.Scan override exists. A `SCAN {root}:dlg:*` loop "as PurgeRoot does" therefore visits a different master per page on Redis Cluster. The treebudget Reserver takes the base client when no per-concern delegation URL is set (cmd/lenny-gateway/session_messaging.go:143; redis_quota.go:151-159 builds a ClusterClient from --redis-cluster-addrs). — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegationtree/treebudget/treebudget.go:474-500
FACT: The shipped Sweeper already does a lease Get + Acquire per non-terminal row per replica every 15 s (coordination.go:303-327; flags.go:529), so per-row per-replica Redis reads are the shipped pattern; the new sweepSuspended HeldLen adds ZCOUNT (+LLEN in durable mode) per suspended row per replica every 5 s tick (watchdog.go:166), before the lease gate. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:137
WATCHOUT: Idle suspension turns the old idle expiry (default = age cap 7200 s, watchdog.go:112,131) into a non-terminal `suspended` row that lives up to 604800 s, so every per-row-per-tick cost now scales with the 7-day suspended population rather than with concurrent sessions. Quantify any per-row sweep against that population.
WATCHOUT: completeResume item 5.2's store-error repeat (added round 10) states no pacing and runs with the delivery lock held. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:76
OPEN: The shipped PurgeRoot (GDPR step 16) has the same Cluster SCAN routing defect. It predates 0092 and is not filed here; whoever owns §12.8 erasure should check it.

### [non-spec.11.review-reliability.1]
FACT: The Sweeper renews a lease only inside its per-tenant `List` loop and returns on a `List` error, so a Postgres outage longer than the 60 s coordination-lease TTL lets every lease lapse; on recovery the first Sweeper to list a `running` row with `PodAssignment` adopts it and `RecordHandoff` advances `coordination_generation` — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:303-305, :342, :375, :404-413; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/sessionserver.go:634
WATCHOUT: CODE-4 item 5.2's commit repeat recognises its own landed commit only at `CoordinationGeneration == G`; a takeover during the outage leaves the committed row at G+1 naming the pod, and item 5.3 then releases a pod the row (and the adopter's binding) names. The round-10 test (non-spec-changes.md:272) pins `ReleaseUncommitted` for a `suspended` row too, which also names the pod. Remedy: on a guard miss, re-read; when `PodAssignment` names the result's sandbox, publish nothing and release nothing.
WATCHOUT: the same repeat has no backoff; any fix should reuse a jittered backoff rather than a hot loop on a failing store.

### [non-spec.12.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: CODE-4 item 5.3's landed test now admits only a row in a state `podHeld` admits (CODE-5 item 1) — BECAUSE "recovery state" was ambiguous (§6.2 means resume_pending/resuming; §15 "recovering" means resume_pending/awaiting_client_action), and a retries-exhausted sweepResuming exit leaves an awaiting_client_action row at G naming the shared concurrent-pool pod, which the exclusion reading treated as landed and so leaked the slot and minted leases — ALTERNATIVES: inline the state list (second copy of podHeld's set); define "recovery state" to include awaiting_client_action (exclusion list must enumerate every non-holding state).
WATCHOUT: the pointer names podHeld's state set only; `podHeld` is an unexported coordination-package helper, so sessionserver must not call it. Do not reintroduce a state exclusion list in item 5.3. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go:785-789 (exhausted exit sets awaiting_client_action only); pkg/gateway/sessionserver/failure.go:337-338 (failure report stamps PodAssignment).

### [non-spec.12.review-fresh.1]
FACT: The spec uses "recovery state(s)" for `resume_pending` and `resuming` only (spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:288), but "recovering state" for `resume_pending` and `awaiting_client_action` (spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:338, spec/15_external-api-surface.md:1637). FACT: `sweepResuming`'s exhausted exit writes `awaiting_client_action` and leaves `PodAssignment` and `coordination_generation` as they are. A failure report stamps the failed pod's sandbox into `PodAssignment`, so on a concurrent pool a `resuming` row can name the same shared pod the new slot is on. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go:780-790; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/failure.go:337-338
WATCHOUT: In CODE-4 item 5.3, the landed test (after a store error: the row is non-terminal, not in a recovery state, and names the result's sandbox) has to exclude `awaiting_client_action` explicitly. Otherwise, on a concurrent pool, a watchdog exhaustion at `G` reads as a landed commit, publishes a binding, and leaks the slot. The safe statement is the CODE-5 `podHeld` state set (`running`, `input_required`, `suspended`). EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:77
FACT: SPEC-1b item 4's inbox-then-DLQ order sentence is replaced by SPEC-5i2 item 1 with a pointer. It is not a live contradiction of SPEC-5i's acceptance-time order. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:70, :495

### [non-spec.12.review-performance.1]
FACT: On a concurrent pool the slot ID is the session ID ("SlotID equals SessionID"; "one session occupies exactly one slot"), and `PodAssignment` holds `SandboxName`, the shared pod name. A second slot of the same session on the same pod would share the first slot's key. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/slotbinder.go:150; pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/slotclaimer.go:211, :774; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/create.go:356
UNVERIFIED: CODE-4 item 5.3 says a guard miss "above G" means a takeover, so it publishes nothing and releases nothing. Suppose a replica loses its Postgres connection for longer than MaxResumingSeconds while its lease lapses (the Sweeper's List fails, so it does not renew; coordination.go:303-305). A peer can then exit A's incarnation, adopt the session, and commit B on the same concurrent pod, and A's repeat misreads B's commit as its own. Whether B can reserve a slot on A's pod while A's same-key slot is live, and so whether A's slot and its minted leases leak, depends on SlotClaimer's handling of a duplicate session ID. Nobody has checked this. The concurrent-pool lost-commit test ("both slots on one pod", non-spec-changes Tier 1 driver bullet) assumes the duplicate is possible, and the implementor should confirm that against slotclaimer.go.
FACT: A coordination lease read that fails already failed closed before this proposal (acquireCoordinationLease aborts on a Redis error, coordination_lease.go:34-45). The Design lease gate's refusal during a Redis outage is therefore no worse than the shipped behaviour.

### [non-spec.12.review-reliability.1]
FACT: Round-11 rewrite of CODE-4 item 5.3 (landed test after a store error) traced against every state a row can reach between a lost commit ack and the repeat: terminal (cancelled) -> ReleaseUncommitted; `suspended` at G -> publish + fence, no tail/delivery; takeover at G+1 -> publish nothing, release nothing; recovery state -> release. No row reaches `running`/`input_required` from the unpublished binding except through the commit itself, because the executor has no binding until step 4. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:76-78.
WATCHOUT: The concurrent-pool case (attempt A's commit not applied, A's store errors outlast the watchdog exit, a peer's attempt B commits on the same shared pod) reads as "landed above G" and releases nothing. Not filed: SlotID == SessionID (pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/slotbinder.go:150; resume_slot_reservation_test.go:80), so A's slot is B's slot and releasing it would tear down B; the scenario also needs a replica-local store fault plus lease migration.
WATCHOUT: sessionserver has no server-lifetime context or Shutdown method today (grep finds none); CODE-4 item 2's "server-lifetime context, which Server cancels on shutdown" is new wiring. Not material: an uncancelled goroutine dies with the process, and the shutdown edge case covers both outcomes.
USEFUL [review-log.md:2048, :2227]: the sweepDLQExpiry stale-list race with the commit's deliverBuffered was already weighed below the bar; it saved re-deriving it.

DECISION: Returned no findings — BECAUSE the only r14→r16 change (DOCS-1b names the client guide's `maxIdleTime` TTL row) resolves against docs/client-guide/session-lifecycle.md:641, and re-tracing the message edges (idle suspension with StampSync refusal and re-route, zero-hold release racing resumeHeldPod via errResumeHeldPodReleased, podless and held-pod resume on and off the coordinating replica, completeResume and resumeHeldAndDrain delivery, backlog resume keyed on SuspendedReason) ends each message delivered at least once with removal after Send, or expired by sweepDLQExpiry in a recovering state or by the terminal drain — ALTERNATIVES: cross-replica clock skew between EnqueuedAt and SuspendedAt in CountAcceptedAfter (not filed: window is the skew, outcome is the settled interrupt-suspension wait-for-client); the client guide's mermaid `suspended --> running : ... delivery:immediate message` edge (not filed: doc-mirror gaps in this loop are refuted as polish, see history line 142, and DOCS-1b's "suspension ... passages" plausibly covers it).
FACT: sweepSuspended's backlog-resume skip of the release cannot pin a pod indefinitely through a resumeHeldPod age-cap refusal, because a suspended row accrues no active age and sweepMaxAge expires an over-cap row on the same tick — EVIDENCE: spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:258 (suspended Paused); non-spec-changes.md:24-29.

### [non-spec.16.review-performance.1]
DECISION: Returned no findings for non-spec round 16 — BECAUSE the only change since r14 is the DOCS-1b `maxIdleTime` TTL-row target, which adds no write, store dependency, or failure path. Re-tracing the session row writes showed that `AccrueSessionAge` rides inside the existing `Store.Update` funnel and adds no write. That holds for `AdvanceLastSeq` too, which already goes through `Update` (cmd/lenny-gateway/session_messaging.go:270-280; pgstore.go:420 is the only `UPDATE sessions`). Every remaining cost lead is either OD-A or a refuted family. — ALTERNATIVES: refiling the Redis-outage path-2 rejection (CODE-4 item 6 Callers plus item 7, "handles its error as an error of that executor.Send"). Rejected for the reasons below.
FACT: Under a Redis outage, a path-2 message to a `running` session now gets EXECUTOR_FAILURE immediately, because the DLQ `PeekOldest` is a Redis ZRANGE (dlq.go:45-57; session_messaging.go:104). Today it is delivered, and the rate limiter fails closed only after 60 s (spec/12_storage-architecture.md:216). The only regression is that first 60 s window, and no message is lost. This is the same family as review-log.md:871. Do not refile it without a loss case.
USEFUL [spec.1.review-performance.1 FACT, Redis-outage DLQ peek]: it settled the main reliability lead again this round.

DECISION: This round reports no test-coverage findings. BECAUSE: tested behavior covers every CODE item.
